RBI Banking and Economy 
 
India's GDP Grows 7.8% in Q1FY27 Despite West Asia Crisis, Manufacturing and Services 
Lead the Way 
 India's economy grew 7.8% in the June quarter (Q1) of FY27, beating most forecasts, 
though lower than 8.6% growth in the previous March quarter of FY26. 
 India remains one of the fastest -growing major economies, ahead of China (4.3%) and 
Indonesia (5.3%) in the June quarter. 
 GDP growth in the same quarter last year (Q1FY26) was 6.9%. 
 Gross Value Added (GVA) growth stood at 8.2%, higher than GDP growth, indicating 
subsidies exceeded indirect taxes during the quarter. 
 Nominal GDP grew 10.3% in Q1FY27 compared to 9.1% in the preceding quarter, hinting 
at rising inflationary pressure. 
 Investment demand picked up strongly at 11.9%, while private spending (7.1%) and 
government spending (4.3%) slowed compared to the previous quarter. 
 Manufacturing output accelerated to 9.2%, while farm output growth slowed to 3.6%, 
possibly due to delayed monsoon. 
 Services sector grew in double digits (10%) for the third consecutive quarter, led by 
financial, real estate, and IT sectors (12.1%). 
 Combined exports of goods and services grew 12% in real terms, while imports contracted 
1.1%, showing external sector resilience. 
 The statistics ministry revised national accounts data for FY23, FY24, and FY26 using the 
new Producer Price Index (PPI) and revised Index of Industrial Production (IIP) with base 
year 2022-23. 
 Q4 FY26 GDP growth was revised upward to 8.6% from 7.8%, taking overall FY26 growth 
to 7.8% from 7.7%. 
 RBI had estimated 7% growth for Q1FY27; forecasts by other agencies ranged between 
6.9% (India Ratings) and 8% (SBI). 
 SBI Capital Markets expects a policy rate hike in FY27 due to strong growth and rising 
inflation. 
 Economists caution that global geopolitical tensions, trade policy uncertainty, and El Niño 
conditions may moderate growth in coming quarters. 
NIIF Raises ₹19,000 Crore in First Close of Second Infrastructure Fund, Surpasses 60% of 
Target 
 The National Investment and Infrastructure Fund (NIIF) has raised ₹19,000 crore (around 
$2 billion) in the first close of its second infrastructure fund. 
 This amount surpasses 60% of the fund's total target size of ₹30,000 crore. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
4 
 
 The first close was anchored by the Government of India and supported by global and 
domestic institutional investors. 
 Global investors include AustralianSuper, CPP Investments, a wholly-owned subsidiary of 
Abu Dhabi Investment Authority (ADIA), Ontario Teachers' Pension Plan, and Temasek. 
 Indian institutions participating include ICICI Bank, HDFC Bank, Axis Bank, Kotak Life 
Insurance, and HDFC Life Insurance. 
 NIIF expects to mobilise an additional ₹9,000 crore ($950 million) in co-investment capital 
alongside the fund. 
 The fund builds on NIIF's first infrastructure fund, which raised ₹16,000 crore ($2.34 
billion). 
 The first fund invested across renewables, transmission, distribution, battery storage, 
roads, ports, logistics, airports, data centres, and smart metering. 
 NIIF aims to connect global and domestic institutional capital with high -quality 
infrastructure businesses in India. 
 Sanjiv Aggarwal is the Managing Director and CEO of NIIF. 
RBI's Net Short Dollar Position in Forward Market Hits Record $136.77 Billion Amid Rupee 
Depreciation Pressure 
 The Reserve Bank of India's (RBI's) outstanding net short dollar position in the forward 
market rose to a record $136.77 billion by July-end, up from $103.33 billion at June-end. 
 Short positions in contracts of less than one year rose to $47.66 billion by July -end from 
$40.33 billion at June-end. 
 Short positions in contracts longer than one year rose to $91.54 billion from $64.21 billion 
during the same period. 
 Of the total $136.77 billion net short position, $15.59 billion was in one -month contracts, 
$7.32 billion in 1 -3 month tenures, $24.75 billion in 3 months to one year, and $91.54 
billion in more than one year contracts. 
 The RBI also held a $2.43 billion long position in one-month contracts. 
 Market participants said the rise in short positions indicates the RBI has been more active 
in the forward market to manage depreciation pressure on the rupee rather than letting 
these positions mature. 
 The rupee depreciated by 0.76% in July. 
 The RBI is likely limiting spot intervention because a large portion of dollars raised through 
the FCNR(B) swap facility are borrowed and must be returned over the next three to five 
years. 
 The RBI is using the NDF (non -deliverable forward) market and futures market to keep 
spot selling of dollars to a minimum. 
 RBI intervention is expected to become more cautious once the FCNR(B) swap window 
closes, as the balance of payments is likely to move closer to neutral in the second half of 
the year. 
 Gaura Sen Gupta is the Chief Economist at IDFC FIRST Bank.

NCDEX Launches RAINCHNNAI Weather Derivatives Contract for Northeast Monsoon 
 NCDEX (National Commodity and Derivatives Exchange) has launched a new rainfall -
based weather derivatives contract called 'RAINCHNNAI' for the Northeast monsoon. 
 This follows the success of 'RAINMUMBAI', India's first rainfall-based weather derivatives 
contract. 
 The Northeast monsoon mainly impacts southern parts of India and accounts for nearly 
70% of Chennai's annual rainfall. 
 RAINCHNNAI contracts will cover the months of September, October, November, and 
December. 
 RAINMUMBAI covers the months of June, July, August, and September. 
 September will be common to both contracts to ensure continuity in hedging without any 
break. 
 The contract has been developed in collaboration with IIT Bombay and is based on official 
data from the India Meteorological Department (IMD). 
 It uses a scientifically structured Cumulative Deviation Rainfall (CDR) model that tracks 
the deviation of actual rainfall from the Long Period Average (LPA) at Chennai's 
Meenambakkam and Nungambakkam stations. 
 RAINMUMBAI uses IMD data from Santacruz and Colaba stations in Mumbai for 
settlement. 
 The contract will have a tick size of 1 millimetre and maximum order lots of 50 lots. 
 The methodology is based on LPA determined from the past 50 years and actual rainfall. 
 Unlike traditional insurance products, these weather derivatives are settled purely on 
observed data, eliminating the need for loss assessment. 
 This enables faster settlement cycles and greater operational efficiency. 
 Kedar Deshpande is the Chief Business Officer of NCDEX. 
 RAINCHNNAI and RAINMUMBAI together complete India's rainfall risk management 
ecosystem and aim to build a new asset class for India's climate economy. 
Services Sector Growth Broadens in June, Real Estate Leads with 24.7% Expansion 
 Eighteen of the 19 sub-sectors in India's services sector recorded growth in June, up from 
16 in May, according to the third print of the trial Index of Services Production (ISP) 
released by the National Statistics Office (NSO). 
 Only air transport remained in contraction, declining 6% in June compared to 2.8% in May, 
marking a third consecutive month of decline. 
 Eight sub-sectors logged double-digit growth in June, unchanged from May but lower than 
14 in April. 
 Two sectors that had contracted in May returned to growth: postal and courier services 
rose 5% (from -1%), and information and broadcasting grew 4.6% (from -7.6%). 
 Real estate was the fastest-growing segment, accelerating to 24.7% in June from 17.7% 
in May. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
6 
 
 Top performers included retail trade (18%), wholesale trade (15.1%), administrative and 
support services (14.4%), and IT and computer-related services (13.5%). 
 Wholesale trade quickened to 15.1% from 3.7% in May, and administrative and support 
services rose to 14.4% from 5%. 
 Retail trade picked up to 18% from 13.3% in May. 
 The sharpest loss of momentum was in accommodation and food, where growth slowed 
to 10.2% from 27.4% in May. 
 Telecommunications eased to 9.5% from 11.8%, and banking edged down to 11.4% from 
12.1%. 
 In index terms, accommodation and food stood highest at 141.2, followed by retail trade 
at 138 and road transport at 131.6. 
 Air transport stood lowest at 91.2, with postal and courier services at 103.2 and railway 
transport at 105.5. 
 The West Asia conflict is straining the services sector by pushing up aviation fuel and food 
costs and disrupting airlines, restaurants, logistics, and travel businesses. 
 The trial ISP, with 2024-25 as the base year, covers nearly 60% of India's services output 
and is the country's first high-frequency monthly measure of services activity, filling a gap 
alongside the Index of Industrial Production (IIP). 
DGFT Aligns INR Export Settlement with Foreign Currency Realisation; Customs Clarifies 
Re-Export Timeline 
 DGFT Notification No. 30/2026-27 dated August 20, 2026 aligns the Foreign Trade Policy 
(FTP) with the INR settlement framework already permitted under FEMA/RBI regulations. 
 Before this notification, Para 2.52 of FTP 2023 allowed export contracts and invoices to 
be denominated in INR but generally required export proceeds to be realised in freely 
convertible currency, subject to specified exceptions. 
 The FEMA (Manner of Receipt and Payment) Regulations, 2023 permit trade transactions 
with countries other than ACU member countries to be settled in INR or foreign currency. 
 RBI has provided specific mechanisms for INR settlement, including Vostro and Special 
Rupee Vostro arrangements. 
 Under Notification No. 30, qualifying export proceeds realised in INR through prescribed 
banking channels are treated at par with export proceeds realised in foreign currency for 
export benefits, incentives, and fulfilment of export obligations. 
 The notification does not expand the underlying FEMA permission to receive INR; it only 
aligns FTP with the existing FEMA/RBI framework. 
 It is more appropriate to describe the notification as an alignment of FTP with FEMA/RBI 
rules rather than a new liberalisation of INR settlement. 
 RBI Master Directions on 'Export of Goods and Services' (Para A1), 'Deposits and 
Accounts' (Part -II), and FEMA (Manner of Receipt & Payment) Regulations, 2023 are 
relevant references. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
7 
 
 Regarding Customs exemption notification 158/95-Cus dated November 14, 1995, the six-
month period for re -export after repairs or reconditioning starts from the date of actual 
Customs clearance of imported goods. 
 CBEC circular no. 14/97 -Cus dated June 3, 1997 clarifies that the six -month period is 
calculated from the date of actual clearance of goods, not from the date of filing the Bill of 
Entry. 
 This clarification ensures that delays in establishing the identity of goods do not defeat the 
purpose of the beneficial notification. 
Net GST Revenue Rises 8.3% in August to ₹1.68 Trillion; Import-Related Collections Surge 
29% 
 Central and state governments collected ₹1.68 trillion in net GST revenue in August, an 
8.3% growth from the same month last year. 
 Gross GST revenue before adjusting for refunds stood at ₹1.99 trillion in August, showing 
a 14.8% annual growth. 
 In July, net GST collections were ₹1.81 trillion after adjusting for ₹29,968 crore of tax 
refunds. 
 GST rates were restructured in September last year to boost consumption demand. 
 Gross domestic GST revenue rose 9.3% year-on-year to ₹1.37 trillion. 
 Gross GST revenue from imports surged 29% to ₹62,604 crore, outpacing domestic 
growth. 
 GST refunds jumped 68% year-on-year to ₹31,795 crore in August. 
 Domestic refunds increased nearly 73% to ₹18,490 crore, while refunds of GST paid on 
imports rose nearly 62% to ₹13,305 crore. 
 Cumulative gross GST collections during April-August of FY27 were up 11% year-on-year 
at ₹10.43 trillion. 
 Net GST collections for the same period rose 9% to ₹8.90 trillion. 
 The rise in import -related GST points to continuing dependence on external sourcing in 
certain segments, reinforcing the need for localisation and import substitution. 
 Experts said faster refund processing is supporting business liquidity while tempering 
headline revenue gains. 
 The GST Council is scheduled to meet in New Delhi on September 12, with an officers' 
meeting on September 11. 
 Experts expect the government to consider strong reform measures at the upcoming GST 
Council meeting amid limited concerns over GST collections. 
Government Revises Petroleum Export Duties from September 1; Diesel Levy Rises, Petrol 
Duty Reintroduced, ATF Cut 
 The government revised export duties on petroleum products with effect from September 
1, 2026. 
 Export duty on diesel has been increased to ₹25 per litre from ₹24 per litre. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
8 
 
 An export duty of ₹1.5 per litre has been imposed on petrol, compared with nil previously. 
 Export levy on aviation turbine fuel (ATF) has been reduced to ₹19 per litre from ₹19.5 per 
litre. 
 The levies on exports of petrol, diesel, and ATF were first introduced from March 27, 2026, 
to ensure domestic availability of petroleum products amid the West Asia crisis. 
 Rates are reviewed and revised every fortnight based on average international prices of 
crude oil, petrol, diesel, and ATF since the previous review. 
 The previous revision took effect on August 15. 
 There is no change in existing excise duty rates on petrol and diesel cleared for domestic 
consumption. 
 India's refiners remain significant exporters of petroleum products, and the government 
uses export levies as a policy tool to balance overseas sales with domestic availability 
amid volatile global oil markets. 
India's Current Account Deficit Widens to $4.2 Billion in Q1FY27 as Trade Gap Grows 
 India's current account deficit (CAD) widened to $4.2 billion, or 0.5% of GDP, in the April-
June quarter of FY27. 
 This compares with a revised CAD of $3.4 billion, or 0.4% of GDP, in the same quarter a 
year ago. 
 The widening was primarily due to a higher merchandise trade deficit, which rose to $86.1 
billion in Q1FY27 from $68.9 billion a year ago. 
 The increase was partly offset by higher net services receipts, which rose to an estimated 
$51.6 billion from $47.9 billion a year ago, led by computer services, business services, 
and transportation. 
 The capital account recorded a net outflow of $3.9 billion in Q1FY27, against an inflow of 
$7.9 billion in the year-ago quarter. 
 Portfolio investment recorded an outflow of $9.6 billion, against an inflow of $1.6 billion a 
year ago. 
 Foreign direct investment (FDI) rose to $6.1 billion from $5.2 billion. 
 Banking capital, including NRI deposits and short -term credit, provided inflows, while 
external commercial borrowing fell to $1 billion from $5.5 billion. 
 With the capital account outflow exceeding the CAD, the overall balance of payments 
recorded a deficit of $8.1 billion in Q1FY27, compared with a surplus of $4.5 billion a year 
ago. 
 Foreign-exchange reserves recorded a valuation loss of $14.4 billion during the quarter, 
compared with a valuation gain of $25.3 billion a year ago, due to changes in gold prices 
and dollar appreciation. 
 In nominal terms, India's foreign-exchange reserves fell by $22.5 billion during April-June, 
compared with an accretion of $29.8 billion in the same quarter a year ago. 
 Aditi Nayar, Chief Economist at Icra, said the CAD was contained at a comfortable 0.5% 
of GDP despite challenges from the West Asia crisis. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
9 
 
 Icra expects the CAD to widen in Q2 and Q3, taking the full -year CAD to around 0.9% of 
GDP, compared with 0.7% in FY26. 
FCNR(B) Inflows Soar to $127.2 Billion Through RBI Swap Window, Far Exceeding Market 
Expectations 
 Banks mobilised $127.2 billion through the FCNR(B) swap window by August 31, taking 
total forex inflows under the RBI facility to $136.4 billion. 
 This far exceeded late market expectations of $90-100 billion. 
 The FCNR(B) window closed on August 31, while facilities for external commercial 
borrowings (ECBs) and overseas foreign currency borrowings (OFCBs) will remain open 
until December 31, 2026. 
 Banks have so far raised $5.3 billion through OFCBs and $3.9 billion through ECBs. 
 FCNR(B) mobilisation accelerated sharply in the final week before the deadline, rising 
from $65.4 billion on August 21. 
 Deposits raised under the scheme have maturities of between three and five years, with 
most inflows in the five-year category. 
 The RBI operationalised the concessional swap facility for fresh FCNR(B) deposits, 
OFCB, and ECB inflows on June 8. 
 The initial deadline for the FCNR(B) window was September 30, 2026, but the RBI brought 
it forward by a month after receiving a strong response. 
 ICICI Bank mobilised $17.9 billion in FCNR(B) deposits through the scheme. 
 State Bank of India exceeded its $10 billion target for FCNR(B) mobilisation. 
 Public sector banks have disbursed $52.8 billion in loans through their IFSC banking units 
at GIFT City, against sanctioned loans of $54.02 billion. 
 ECBs disbursed by IFSC banking units totalled $11.62 billion between April and August, 
while Indian banks raised $11.12 billion through bond issuances on IFSC exchanges. 
 Core liquidity in the banking system had risen to more than ₹8 trillion by August 15 and 
could exceed ₹10 trillion in September. 
 The RBI is likely to deploy durable liquidity-absorption measures to contain the surplus. 
 Market participants estimate a potential fiscal cost, with cumulative indirect fiscal cost 
potentially exceeding ₹1 trillion. 
 The RBI's net short dollar position in the forward market rose to a record $136.77 billion 
at the end of July, from $103.33 billion at the end of June. 
 Short positions with maturities of more than one year rose to $91.54 billion from $64.21 
billion over the same period. 
 The scale of inflows could intensify competition among banks for high -quality borrowers, 
potentially leading to lower loan yields and some moderation in margins. 
 Madhavi Arora is Chief Economist at Emkay Global Financial Services, and Sachin 
Sachdeva is Vice-President and Co-Group Head, Financial Sector Ratings, at ICRA. 
GIFT-IFSC Banks Sanction $54 Billion Under RBI's FCNR(B) Swap Facility by August-End 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
10 
 
 Twenty international banking units (IBUs) at GIFT -IFSC sanctioned $54.02 billion under 
the RBI's special swap facility for FCNR(B) deposits as of August 31. 
 Of the total sanctioned amount, $52.82 billion has been disbursed. 
 Sanctions rose from $28.60 billion on August 14 to $37.26 billion on August 21 and further 
to $54.02 billion by August 31. 
 Participating IBUs include both domestic and foreign banks. 
 The facility was part of the RBI's measures to mobilise foreign currency inflows through 
FCNR(B) deposits. 
 The FCNR(B) window closed on August 31, while the facility for external commercial 
borrowings (ECBs) and overseas foreign currency borrowings remains open until 
December 31. 
 IBUs disbursed $11.62 billion through ECBs between April and August 2026. 
 Monthly ECB disbursements rose from $1.54 billion in April to $3.54 billion in August. 
 Indian banks raised $11.12 billion through bond listings on IFSC exchanges during April -
August, of which $9.17 billion was raised in July and August alone. 
 IFSCA said IBUs at GIFT -IFSC are being used to mobilise funds from multiple 
jurisdictions, including the UK, US, Mexico, West Asia, Hong Kong, Singapore, and some 
African countries. 
 The development follows the finance ministry's direction to public sector banks to use the 
financial services ecosystem and institutional infrastructure at GIFT -IFSC for 
implementing the RBI's swap facilities for FCNR(B) deposits and ECBs. 
NBFCs Tell RBI That Revolving Credit Ban Will Impact a Larger Section of Industry 
 The Finance Industry Development Council (FIDC), a self -regulatory organisation for 
shadow banks, told the RBI that the proposed ban on revolving credit offered by NBFCs 
would impact a larger section of players and products than earlier estimated. 
 FIDC and NBFC representatives met RBI Deputy Governor S C Murmu and shared their 
feedback. 
 Around 30 -40 lenders submitted their responses to FIDC; all suggestions will be 
consolidated and sent to the RBI through the Confederation of Indian Industry (CII). 
 An NBFC chief executive said the RBI may look at a partial restriction rather than a blanket 
ban. 
 A key concern for lenders is the treatment of products that allow limited redraw or 
replenishment within an existing sanctioned facility, which NBFCs argue are not the kind 
of revolving lending the RBI seeks to restrict. 
 Supply chain finance is another area where NBFCs have sought an exemption or specific 
clarification, as such financing is closely monitored through GST data and loans are 
disbursed directly to suppliers. 
 The RBI's proposal aims to ensure repayment discipline and address concerns around 
borrower creditworthiness and loan evergreening. 
 NBFCs said a broad definition could capture legitimate lending products. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
11 
 
 The final guidelines on revolving credit could take another two months as the RBI 
assesses feedback. 
 NBFCs have also sought clarity on whether recovery calls can be made through the 1600-
number series mandated for service and transactional calls by regulated financial entities. 
 The Telecom Regulatory Authority of India (Trai) has mandated regulated financial entities 
to use the 1600 series for service and transactional voice calls. 
 NBFCs said the framework could create difficulties in recovery if borrowers do not answer 
or block calls from these numbers. 
 Lenders also want clarity on whether they can use regular numbers after unsuccessful 
attempts through the 1600 series. 
Private Banks’ Deposit Growth Outpaces PSBs by Nearly 350 bps in June 2026 
 According to the latest RBI data, deposits in private sector banks grew by 13.8%, 
significantly higher than the 10.3% growth recorded by public sector banks (PSBs) at the 
end of June 2026. 
 Overall deposits of scheduled commercial banks grew by 11.5% YoY, while bank credit 
grew at a much faster pace of 16.5%, widening the gap between credit and deposit growth. 
 Term deposits grew by 12.9%, outperforming savings deposits (10.6%) and current 
deposits (5.3%). 
 Bank credit growth was broad -based, with credit to the private corporate sector growing 
by 21.1%, while credit to the public sector and households grew by 14.6% and 15.2%, 
respectively. 
 Among major sectors, credit growth was strong in finance (22.4%), trade (18.1%), industry 
(15.5%), agriculture (15.1%), and personal loans (12.7%). 
UPI Records All-Time High of 24.51 Billion Transactions in August 2026 
 UPI transactions reached a record 24.51 billion in August 2026, with a total transaction 
value of ₹29.82 trillion, according to data released by the National Payments Corporation 
of India (NPCI). 
 Transaction volume increased by 3.6% month -on-month and 22% year -on-year, while 
transaction value grew 20% year-on-year. 
 The average number of daily UPI transactions increased to 791 million in August, 
compared with 763 million in July. 
 IMPS transactions declined to 360 million in August from 364 million in July, while their 
total value stood at ₹7.04 trillion. 
 FASTag transactions rose 1.2% to 351 million, while AePS transactions increased 4% to 
104 million during August 2026. 
UPI International Enables Indian Donations to Nepal’s Disaster Relief Fund 
 The Embassy of Nepal in India has encouraged Indian donors to activate UPI International 
to contribute to Nepal’s Prime Minister Disaster Relief Fund amid devastating floods. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
12 
 
 This is among the first instances of UPI International being used for a foreign government’s 
disaster relief appeal, expanding its use beyond cross -border tourist and merchant 
payments. 
 Donors need to activate the UPI International feature on their UPI or banking applications 
and use an authorised online payment gateway of the Government of Nepal. 
 Once activated for a country, users can transact in that country’s currency for one week, 
with the payment amount converted from Indian rupees to Nepalese rupees. 
 NPCI International Payments Limited (NIPL) is responsible for globalising UPI, while NPCI 
operates the UPI network; cross -border UPI payments between India and Nepal were 
launched in March 2024 in partnership with Fonepay Payment Service. 
PhonePe Launches UPI 123Pay for Feature Phone Users 
 PhonePe launched UPI 123Pay, enabling feature phone users to make UPI transactions 
without internet access. 
 The service uses an SMS-based system and can operate even on low or 2G networks. 
 It supports peer-to-peer and peer-to-merchant payments, balance checks and transaction 
history. 
 The service also includes an AI-powered voice helpline available in English and 12 Indian 
languages. 
Razorpay and IndusInd Bank Launch AI Account Manager ‘RAY’ 
 Razorpay, in partnership with IndusInd Bank, launched ‘RAY’, a conversational AI Account 
Manager on WhatsApp for businesses. 
 The platform enables MSMEs to manage payments, transactions, settlements and 
customer support through a chat-based interface. 
 Users can analyse transactions, generate payment links, issue refunds and resolve 
queries without using traditional dashboards. 
 RAY has already recorded over 20,000 conversations, with Bajaj Finserv among its first 
live users. 
 
SBI to Use UPI Transaction Data for Lending to Small Businesses Without GST 
Registration 
Key Points 
• State Bank of India (SBI), India's largest lender, is developing a lending solution that will 
use Unified Payments Interface (UPI) transaction data as a proxy for sales to extend loans 
to small businesses that do not have Goods and Services Tax (GST) registration. 
• SBI currently processes business loans in about 10 minutes for customers with GST 
registration, PAN, and other required data, and has disbursed ₹1 trillion in such loans over 
the past one-and-a-half years. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
13 
 
• The bank aims to build a business profile using UPI transaction data and other digital 
payment behaviour, subject to customer consent, to assess businesses whose revenues 
are not otherwise regularly documented. 
• This marks a broader shift in bank lending from balance -sheet-based financing toward 
cash-flow-based lending, provided the cash flow is visible through digital channels rather 
than physical cash. 
• For GST-registered businesses, a physical site visit remains the main manual step in SBI's 
automated lending process, retained to verify the existence of a shop or business 
premises and prevent misuse. 
• The approach is aimed at addressing the funding gap in the Micro, Small and Medium 
Enterprises (MSME) sector, particularly among micro businesses with limited financial 
records or savings. 
• SBI is also exploring wallets to handle small transactions of up to ₹100 in order to reduce 
load on banking infrastructure, along with voice-based, multilingual and AI-driven services 
to simplify loan applications for customers. 
RBI Proposes SOP for Temporary Debit Holds on Suspected Money Mule Accounts 
Key Points 
• The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) has proposed a Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) 
for banks to place temporary debit holds on transactions or accounts suspected of money-
mule activity and cyber -enabled financial fraud, following a Supreme Court order dated  
August 4 directing the central bank to prescribe such a procedure. 
• Under the proposed SOP, banks must immediately place a temporary debit hold on a 
transaction identified as suspicious; if the entire account is flagged, the hold can be 
extended to the account. 
• A suspected money mule transaction is defined as one of ₹1,000 or more flagged by 
banks' AI/ML -based transaction -monitoring systems, based on indicators such as 
transactions unusual for the customer's profile or links to an account already reported as 
fraudulent. 
• Banks must immediately inform customers about the hold, its reasons, the process for 
removal, and the contact details of the concerned officer; customers get 20 days to submit 
an explanation. 
• Banks must decide within 10 days of receiving the customer's explanation, or within 30 
days of the hold if no explanation is received; if satisfied, the hold must be removed 
immediately, and if not, the matter is reported to police via the National Cybercr ime 
Reporting Portal's Citizen Financial Cyber Fraud Reporting and Management System 
(NCRP-CFCFRMS). 
• If no law enforcement instruction is received within 30 days of this reference, the bank 
must remove the hold on the 31st day; the maximum duration of a hold, absent such 
instruction, is capped at 60 days from the initial hold date. 
• RBI has proposed that account -level holds be used only as a last resort in exceptional 
circumstances, and banks must still file Suspicious Transaction Reports (STRs) with the 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
14 
 
Financial Intelligence Unit-India (FIU-IND); failure to file an STR after identifying a money 
mule account will be deemed non-compliance with Know Your Customer (KYC) directions. 
• Banks must maintain centralised records of such cases for at least five to ten years after 
account closure, designate nodal officers for grievance redressal, and resolve customer 
complaints within 30 days. 
• The RBI has invited public and stakeholder comments by October 2, with the directions 
proposed to take effect from April 1, 2027. 
Government Paves Way for Banks to Levy Charges on UPI Transactions Above ₹2,000 
Key Points 
• The government issued a notification under the Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 
2007, specifying that the "zero -charge" mandate on electronic payments applies only to 
RuPay-powered debit cards and UPI transactions up to ₹2,000. 
• The notification states that "no bank or system provider shall impose, whether directly or 
indirectly, any charge upon a person making or receiving a payment by using the electronic 
modes of payment" for these specified instruments up to the ₹2,000 thresho ld, implying 
banks may now levy charges on UPI transactions above this amount. 
• The final decision on whether banks impose charges at or above this threshold, and the 
exact rate, rests with the UPI and Services Steering Committee headed by the National 
Payments Corporation of India (NPCI). 
• Merchant Discount Rate (MDR) is the fee merchants pay to banks and payment service 
providers for processing digital payment transactions; currently, merchants pay no MDR 
on UPI payments, unlike on debit or credit card transactions. 
• The government had earlier clarified that any MDR on UPI would apply only to a "limited 
set of merchant transactions over a certain threshold," at a fee lower than that charged on 
credit and debit cards.

UPI Payments Above ₹2,000 to Attract 0.4% MDR from October 15, 2026 
Key Points 
• NPCI has announced a Merchant Discount Rate (MDR) of 0.4% on UPI person -to-
merchant (P2M) transactions above ₹2,000, effective October 15, 2026. 
• This is the first charge on UPI since the government made it free in 2020. 
• Who pays: Merchants, not consumers. UPI remains free for individual users. 
• Who benefits:  Banks, UPI apps (PhonePe, Google Pay, Paytm), acquiring banks, 
payment service provider banks, and payment aggregators — the fee is shared across 
the payments chain. 
• Cap: MDR is capped at ₹300 for transactions of ₹75,000 or more. 
• Exemptions: 
 P2M transactions up to ₹2,000 — free 
 Peer-to-peer (P2P) transfers (family/friends) — free 
 P2PM (small vendors receiving up to ₹1 lakh/month via UPI) — free 
 UPI AutoPay (recurring payments like utility bills, OTT subscriptions, SIPs) — free 
• Special rates: 
 Flat ₹5 for railways, telecom, insurance, fuel, electricity, municipal water, piped 
natural gas (for transactions above ₹2,000) 
 0.02% for capital market transactions (mutual funds, stocks, broker wallet top-ups), 
capped at ₹300 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
17 
 
 Education fee payments — flat fee or capped rates for transactions above ₹2,000 
• Consumer protection: UPI apps cannot levy platform fees or any charges on customers 
for UPI transactions. 
• Dedicated fund: NPCI to create a ~₹700 crore/year fund for small merchants to boost 
digital payment infrastructure in Tier III–VI regions, North-East, J&K, and Ladakh. Details 
to be finalised with RBI in 3 months. 
• Timeline: Effective October 15, 2026, giving banks, fintechs, and payment aggregators 
time to upgrade systems. 
UPI Transaction Split 
• P2P: 70% of UPI value 
• P2M: 30% of UPI value 
• 67% of P2M value comes from transactions above ₹2,000 
India's Trade Deficit Eases to 5-Month Low in August on Robust Export Growth 
Key Points 
• India's trade deficit narrowed to a 5-month low of $26.86 billion in August, down from $32 
billion in July and $27.22 billion a year ago. 
• Goods trade: 
 Exports rose 26% YoY to $43.81 billion — fastest growth since June 2022. 
 Imports rose 14% YoY to $70.67 billion. 
• Services trade: 
 Services exports estimated at $38.87 billion, up nearly 25% YoY. 
 Services imports estimated at $21.42 billion, up 37% YoY. 
 Services trade surplus estimated at $17.45 billion. 
 RBI to release final services data later this month. 
• Sector-wise export growth: 
 Engineering goods: up 24% to $12.32 billion 
 Electronic goods: nearly doubled to $5.55 billion 
 Petroleum products: up 63% to $6.81 billion 
 Organic & inorganic chemicals: up 16% to $2.80 billion 
• Sector-wise import growth: 
 Crude oil: up 26% to $16.69 billion 
 Electronic goods: up 41% to $13.66 billion 
 Coal, coke & briquettes: up 25% to $2.57 billion 
 Fertiliser: up 14% to $1.89 billion 
 Gold: fell 58% to $2.30 billion (after Customs duty hike in May) 
• Top trade partners:

 China — largest import source, imports up 17% to $12.78 billion; exports to China 
up 52% to $1.84 billion 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
18

 Trade deficit: $147 billion 
• Export share in GDP: 
 Rose to 14% in Q1 FY27, recovering to FY23 levels.

global demand and US tariffs. 
• Government view: Commerce Secretary Rajesh Agrawal attributed export growth to both

UPI Apps Oppose Platform Fee Ban on Bill Payments Under New MDR Norms 
Key Points 
• Third-party UPI apps have objected to the ban on platform fees under the new MDR 
framework. 
• Reason for objection: Apps say they will lose money on every bill payment processed 
through UPI. 
• Current practice: UPI apps currently charge users a platform fee of ₹3–5 on bill payments 
(electricity, water, gas, credit card bills) through Bharat Connect (formerly BBPS). 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
20 
 
• New framework (effective October 15):  NPCI has banned UPI app providers from 
charging any platform fee or other charge on UPI payments. 
• Problem for apps: 
 Bill payments above ₹2,000 carry a flat MDR of ₹5 per transaction, of which UPI 
apps get only ₹1. 
 Bill payments of ₹2,000 or less carry zero MDR, so apps earn nothing. 
 Apps also pay commission to BBPS, which is higher than their MDR share. 
 Result: Apps spend more on each bill payment than they earn. 
• App strategy: Apps hope to offset losses on bill payments through their share of 0.4% 
MDR on other P2M payments above ₹2,000. 
• Objection raised at: NPCI's UPI Services and Steering Committee meeting on Tuesday. 
• Scope of ban: Covers all UPI payments, including bills of ₹2,000 or less which carry no 
MDR. 
Key Numbers 
• ₹3–5: platform fee currently charged by UPI apps 
• October 15: new MDR framework takes effect 
• 0.4%: MDR on P2M payments above ₹2,000 
• ₹5: flat MDR on bill payments above ₹2,000 
• Zero: MDR on bill payments of ₹2,000 or less 
RBI Absorbs ₹50,000 Crore Through Bond Sale Amid Liquidity Surge 
Key Points 
• RBI sold ₹50,000 crore worth of government securities through an Open Market Operation 
(OMO) sale auction on Thursday. 
• Purpose: To drain durable liquidity (excess money) from the banking system. 
• Part of larger plan:  This was the first of three OMO tranches announced last week to 
suck out ₹1 trillion (₹1 lakh crore) in September. 
• Market response: 
 Demand was robust despite the cut-off price being higher than expected. 
 Some banks preferred buying bonds at current levels over parking funds through 
RBI's Variable Rate Reverse Repo (VRRR) auctions. 
 Benchmark 10-year government bond yield settled at 7.05%, flat against previous 
close. 
• Liquidity position: 
 Net liquidity in the banking system was in surplus of ₹7.37 trillion on Wednesday, 
down from ₹9.85 trillion on Tuesday. 
 Durable liquidity as of August 31 was ₹10.66 trillion, estimated to have risen to ₹14 
trillion by mid-September. 
• Call rate: 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
21 
 
 Overnight Weighted Average Call Rate (WACR) settled at 5.05%, near the 
Standing Deposit Facility (SDF) rate, against previous close of 5.02%. 
 RBI aims to align WACR with the policy repo rate of 5.25% by draining excess 
liquidity. 
• Why OMO instead of VRRR: 
 Longer-tenure VRRR auctions saw muted demand; banks were reluctant to park 
funds for 30 days. 
 RBI turned to OMO sales as a fresh instrument after VRRR auctions failed to draw 
adequate demand. 
• Outlook: RBI is expected to conduct more OMO sales in October to absorb durable 
liquidity. 
STT Collections Surge 53% to ₹40,214 Crore in FY27 After Rate Hike 
Key Points 
• STT collections surged nearly 53% to ₹40,214.36 crore in FY27 (as of September 17), 
from ₹26,305.72 crore in the same period last year. 
• Reason: Hike in STT rates effective from April 1, announced in the Union Budget. 
• Revised STT rates (from April 1): 
 Futures: increased to 0.05% of traded value from 0.02% 
 Options (premium turnover): increased to 0.15% from 0.1% 
 Exercised options: increased to 0.15% of intrinsic value from 0.125% 
 This was the second STT hike since 2024. 
• Past collections: 
 FY26: ₹57,522 crore 
 FY25: ₹53,296 crore 
 FY27 Budget projection: ₹73,700 crore 
• What STT covers: Securities trades on recognised stock exchanges, including equities, 
futures and options, and equity-oriented mutual funds. 
• How STT is levied: 
 Applicable on all sell transactions for futures and options 
 Futures trade valued at actual traded price 
 Option trade valued at premium 
 On exercise of option contract: STT levied on settlement price of the day if it is an 
in-the-money contract 
• Nature of tax: STT forms part of the government's direct tax collections. 
• Expert view: Deven Choksey, MD at DRChoksey FinServ, said the primary catalyst is the 
government's hike on derivative transactions, meaning significantly more revenue per 
contract traded. Despite Sebi's regulatory tightening (larger contract sizes, stricter position 
limits) to cool retail speculation, overall market transaction values remain very high. 
• Recent developments: 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
22 
 
 Income Tax Department asked stock brokers in March to remit STT collected from 
clients for 2023-24 and preceding years, along with interest, if unpaid. 
 Derivatives volumes under pressure over past month due to regulatory changes 
like closing auction session and 100% collateral requirement. 
FCNR(B) May Generate ₹5 Trillion Notional Profit for Banks Over 5 Years: SBI 
Key Points 
• Banks mobilised $127 billion through FCNR(B) deposits in less than three months before 
RBI advanced the closure of the deposit window. 
• SBI Research estimates: 
 Notional profit of around ₹5 trillion for banks over five years. 
 Could support around ₹25 trillion of additional bank credit. 
• Income calculation: 
 At 7.5% yield, FCNR(B) deposits could generate about ₹1.8 trillion in income each 
year. 
 Interest outgo on deposits: around ₹75,000 crore per year. 
 Effective net interest margin: about ₹1 trillion annually, or ₹5 trillion over five years. 
• Cost estimates: 
 Additional interest cost: around ₹1.75 trillion 
 Foreign-exchange depreciation cost: ₹3.18 trillion (based on assumed 5% annual 
rupee depreciation over five years) 
• RBI's special dollar-rupee swap facility: 
 Designed to hedge exchange-rate risk on deposits. 
 Once principal exchange-rate risk is hedged, subsequent rupee depreciation does 
not create additional contractual loss for banks or RBI. 
 Cumulative hedging cost of $127 billion estimated at around $15 billion (assuming 
3% average annual dollar -rupee hedging cost; deposits divided into 1, 3, and 5 -
year maturity buckets). 
• RBI's gain: 
 Investing $100 billion in globally investible avenues at 4% yield over five years 
could generate around $20 billion. 
 After $15 billion hedging cost, surplus of around $5 billion (₹50,000 crore) for RBI's 
balance sheet. 
 Overall notional profit: ₹5 trillion to banks and ₹0.5 trillion to RBI. 
• Liquidity absorption: Large inflow from the scheme could be absorbed through festive 
season demand, credit -disbursement pipelines, new loan sanctions, and government -
related outflows like advance taxes and GST.

No MDR Charges on UPI Transactions up to ₹2,000 
• The Ministry of Finance  notified that banks and payment system providers cannot 
impose direct or indirect charges on UPI transactions up to ₹2,000. 
• The notification also covers payments made through RuPay-powered debit cards. 
• The decision was issued under Section 10A of the Payment and Settlement Systems 
Act, 2007.

receiving eligible UPI and RuPay debit-card payments.

to ₹2,000.

• Outstanding NRI deposits rose to $200.89 billion at the end of July 2026, from $167.86 
billion in July 2025 and $168.51 billion in June 2026, and the deposit -wise picture was 
as follows:  
 FCNR(B): inflows jumped to $34.53 billion from $772 million, and the outstanding 
amount rose to $68.28 billion in July. 
 NRE: inflows fell to $207 million from $2.42 billion, and the outstanding amount 
stood at $98.02 billion, down from $102.03 billion in July 2025. 
 NRO: inflows were $1.51 billion  against $1.47 billion , and the outstanding 
amount stood at $34.59 billion. 
• Outward remittances by residents under the Liberalised Remittance Scheme (LRS) rose 
4.7% year-on-year to $9.37 billion  in April -July, led by overseas investment  despite 
weaker spending on travel and education:  
 Remittances for equity and debt investment doubled to $1.4 billion , deposit-
related remittances rose 39.3% to $330.32 million, and those for buying property 
abroad rose 13.34% to $185.33 million. 
 Travel-related remittances fell 1.31% to $5.27 billion , and overseas education 
remittances dropped 30.6% to $472.66 million. 
 In July alone, remittances rose 5.3% to $2.45 billion  on higher overseas 
investment and international travel.

RBI advisory panel proposes raising aggregate WMA limit for states by 11.2% to ₹67,839 
crore, flags year-end borrowing 
Key Points 
• An RBI advisory committee has recommended raising the aggregate Ways and Means 
Advances (WMA) limit  for state governments by 11.2% to ₹67,839 crore  from the 
existing ₹61,008 crore , and has also called for greater discipline in the timing of state 
borrowings.  
 The WMA facility lets states meet temporary mismatches between cash inflows 
and expenditure, and the committee said it should not become a source of 
financing budget deficits. 
• The committee's recommendations on setting the limit are as follows:  
 Limits should be reviewed annually based on the latest three years' accounts 
data. 
 The yearly increase should generally be capped at 4%. 
 A state's revised limit should not be lower than its existing limit. 
• The aggregate WMA limit has risen over the past decade: ₹32,225 crore (February 
2016), ₹41,893 crore (April 2020) , ₹47,010 crore (April 2022) , ₹60,118 crore (July 
2024) and ₹61,008 crore (January 2026). 
• The committee flagged the concentration of state borrowing in January-March, which 
can flood the market with state development loans (SDLs) at once, pressure borrowing 
costs and leave states with high cash balances at year-end, and it recommended that 
states spread borrowing evenly across the year.  
 It urged wider adoption of the Benchmark Issuance Strategy, under which states 
build benchmark-sized issuances  to improve liquidity in their securities, and 
most states have adopted it from FY 2026-27. 
• Proposed changes to overdraft rules:  
 Maximum continuous overdraft period cut to 10 working days from 14. 
 Maximum overdraft days in a calendar quarter cut to 30 from 36. 
• Proposed changes on the Consolidated Sinking Fund (CSF):  
 The share of CSF investments  usable to access the RBI's Standing Deposit 
Facility (SDF) raised to 75% from 50% , to encourage states to build CSF and 
Guarantee Redemption Fund (GRF) balances. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
28 
 
 States should gradually raise these balances to at least 5% of outstanding 
marketable debt or guarantees , and states not yet in the CSF and GRF  
arrangements should join. 
RBI clarifies novation rules for OTC derivative contracts, MTM value to be exchanged 
upfront 
Key Points 
• The Reserve Bank of India  on September 22, 2026  clarified that the mark-to-market 
(MTM) value of an OTC derivative contract must be exchanged upfront between the 
transferor and transferee  when the contract is novated, and that any subsequent 
novation after a central counterparty (CCP)  has accepted the contract for settlement 
will follow the RBI's revised directions.  
 The clarifications are part of the RBI's response to feedback on its draft 
directions on novation of OTC derivative contracts. 
• The directions apply to novation undertaken after the date of issuance , covering 
outstanding contracts on that date as well as contracts entered into later.  
 Novation by a CCP for the purpose of settlement is not covered by the directions, 
but any subsequent novation  of a contract accepted by the CCP must follow 
them. 
• The definition of "remaining party" now includes a market maker or a CCP, and where 
the CCP is the remaining party, novation follows the procedure specified by the CCP. 
• The RBI rejected a proposal  to allow novation where both transferor and transferee 
are users, including a user moving a contract to its central treasury or group entity , 
saying one party must be a market maker or a CCP authorised by the RBI. 
• For novations arising from mergers, demergers or amalgamations , the existing 
framework under the RBI's December 9, 2013 circular on "Novation of OTC Derivative 
Contracts" continues to apply. 
• The definition of market maker  is now linked to the governing directions for the 
respective OTC derivative contract , instead of the common definition in the draft 
directions. 
NPCI says about 75% of merchants will not face the new 0.4% UPI levy on transactions 
above ₹2,000 
Key Points 
• NPCI MD & CEO Dilip Asbe  said that about 75% of India's roughly 60 million digital -
payment-accepting merchants have never recorded a transaction above ₹2,000 , so 
they are insulated from the proposed levy on certain UPI payments.  
 The levy is a 0.4% merchant discount rate (MDR)  on UPI transactions above 
₹2,000 made to select merchants, notified by NPCI and effective from October 15. 
 An MDR is a fee the merchant pays to accept digital payments, and it is not 
charged to customers. Peer-to-peer (P2P) transactions remain free of MDR. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
29 
 
• Traders and business associations have announced a "No UPI Day" on October 2 in 
protest, arguing that low margins make a recurring cost of accepting digital payments hard 
to bear, and the Opposition Congress party called the levy a 'UPI tax' and urged the 
government to withdraw it. 
• Steps to prevent the cost from reaching consumers, per Asbe and a finance ministry 
official:  
 Asbe said 80-90% of MDR collections are expected to come from merchants who 
already accept MDR -bearing instruments , and that banks, payment 
aggregators, acquiring banks and NPCI will ensure charges are not passed on 
to customers. 
 The government will work with the Indian Banks' Association (IBA)  to ensure 
merchants do not pass on MDR, and the IBA has been asked to run a campaign 
against misinformation on MDR. 
• The 18% GST on MDR charges may get relief:  
 The Centre expects the GST Council , chaired by Union Finance Minister 
Nirmala Sitharaman  and including state finance ministers , to consider 
lowering the 18% rate on UPI MDR charges. 
 Only the GST Council can decide GST rates, and the official did not say whether 
the issue is on the agenda for the Council's October 7 meeting. 
 As UPI transactions are services, MDR attracts GST, but GST-registered 
businesses can claim input tax credit (ITC) on the tax paid. 
• UPI spending in August by category, per NPCI data:  
 Groceries and supermarkets:  ₹86,882 crore across 3.99 billion transactions, 
the largest category. 
 Debt collection agencies: ₹73,983 crore across 214.98 million transactions. 
 Securities brokers and dealers:  ₹63,667 crore  across 84.12 million  
transactions. 
Govt cuts FY27 gross market borrowing to ₹16 trillion; to raise ₹7.86 trillion through dated 
securities in H2 
Key Points 
• The Centre's borrowing calendar issued on September 25, 2026  shows it will raise 
₹7.86 trillion through dated securities in H2 (October -March) of FY27. Adding about 
₹8.14 trillion raised in H1 , gross market borrowing  is likely to be about ₹16 trillion, 
around ₹1.2 trillion below the Budget Estimate of ₹17.2 trillion.  
 Gross borrowing had already eased to ₹16.09 trillion in February , after the 
government switched securities worth ₹1.11 trillion to roll over debt redemption, 
which also cut the FY27 repayment estimate to ₹4.36 trillion from ₹5.47 trillion. 
 Net market borrowing, which finances the fiscal deficit, is unchanged at ₹11.73 
trillion, as estimated in the Budget. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
30 
 
• The H2 borrowing plan  comes after a 25 basis point rate hike by the US Federal 
Reserve hardened bond yields, and India's 10-year benchmark yield closed at 7.12% 
on Friday.  
 The government has shifted the plan towards longer-tenor securities: the share 
of 15-year bonds rises to 17.6% from 14.5%  in H1, and the shares of 30-, 40- 
and 50-year securities also increase. 
 The shares of 5-year (12.1%), 10-year (26.3%) and 3-year (6.9%) securities fall 
from 15.4%, 29% and 8.1%  in H1, while the 7-year share rises to 9.1% from 
8.1%. 
 A finance ministry official said the longer -tenor focus will raise the weighted 
average maturity (WAM), which fell in H1, and so reduce roll-over risk. 
• How the plan will be executed:  
 Borrowing will be carried out through 23 weekly auctions of ₹33,000-36,000 crore 
each, and the government also aims to raise ₹15,000 crore through green bonds 
in H2. 
 The RBI has fixed the Ways and Means Advances (WMA) limit for H2 at 
₹50,000 crore to handle temporary mismatches in government accounts. 
 The government will continue switching and buying back securities  to smooth 
the redemption profile. 
 For Q3 (October-December), it plans to borrow ₹2.99 trillion through treasury 
bills. 
• The government has shifted its fiscal anchor from the deficit to the debt-to-GDP ratio, 
targeting 49-51% by 2030-31, while central government debt stands at 58.2% of GDP 
against the annual aim of 56.1%.

GIFT City Dual-Listing Plan Gains Pace as Sebi and IFSCA Hold Talks with Stakeholders 
 Plans to allow listed Indian companies to raise capital from global investors through dual 
listing at GIFT City are gathering pace. 
 Sebi (Securities and Exchange Board of India) and IFSCA (International Financial 
Services Centres Authority) held multiple meetings last month to work out the framework. 
 Discussions are focusing on key modalities including potential changes to Sebi regulations 
governing listing, insider trading, buybacks, and takeovers. 
 A committee including members of both regulators and market infrastructure institutions 
(MIIs) is discussing the measures. 
 Companies may be allowed to raise capital from non -residents and global investors in 
dollar denominations. 
 Domestic investors may not be allowed to trade in these securities listed on international 
exchanges at GIFT City. 
 Sebi has raised concerns about price variations and minimum public float before allowing 
dual listings. 
 Authorities are examining how capital raised will be monitored, with custodians at GIFT 
City likely to play a key role. 
 Other areas under consideration include minimum public float, currency fluctuations, depth 
of investor participation, and tax implications. 
 Indian listed companies must maintain at least 25% minimum public shareholding under 
the domestic framework; IFSC has its own minimum public offer requirements. 
 Shares held by investors on the IFSC platform may not count toward the domestic public 
shareholding threshold. 
 The free float of a dual -listed company could get fragmented across two independently 
measured pools. 
 The discussions follow the direct listing framework announced in 2023 and implemented 
in 2024. 
 Sebi's regulations may take primacy as companies are already listed on domestic 
exchanges, requiring robust data sharing and surveillance between both regulators. 
 Key challenges include alignment of depositories, seamless transfer of securities, price 
arbitrage, simultaneous offers with common offer documents, and coordination between 
Sebi and IFSCA. 
 Tax-related matters fall outside Sebi's purview and will need clarification from the 
government at GIFT-IFSC. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
35 
 
 The foreign-currency nature of the IFSC market raises FEMA, taxation, and accounting 
considerations. 
 IFSCA proposed allowing companies to list equity shares directly on GIFT City stock 
exchanges without any public offer. 
 Under the draft framework, unlisted issuers need at least one of three criteria: minimum 
operating revenue of $20 million, pre -tax profit of $1 million, or post -listing market 
capitalisation of $50 million. 
 The first IPO at GIFT City, by XED Executive Development, was withdrawn in March due 
to low subscriptions over KYC concerns. 
 Sources said the factors leading to low subscription have now been resolved, with certain 
GIFT-IFSC players now allowed to underwrite issues. 
 US-based Tryfacta Inc is awaiting IFSCA approval for its IPO, estimated at $100 -150 
million. 
Irdai Proposes Public Insurance Registry to Bridge Data Gaps and Widen Insurance 
Coverage 
 Irdai (Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India) released a consultation 
paper to create a Public Insurance Registry (PIR). 
 The PIR is designed as a population-scale digital infrastructure to bridge information gaps 
and reduce friction among insurers, intermediaries, reinsurers, financial institutions, 
government agencies, and policyholders. 
 The registry aims to accelerate financial inclusion across the country. 
 In March 2026, Irdai had proposed setting up the PIR as a consent -driven, legally 
compliant digital infrastructure covering the entire policy lifecycle from issuance to claims, 
grievance redressal, and dispute resolution. 
 The framework is envisaged under the Sabka Bima Sabki Raksha (Amendment of 
Insurance Laws) Act, 2025. 
 The PIR will operate as an interoperable access layer rather than a single centralised data 
repository. 
 Underlying data will securely reside with original source institutions, while the registry will 
provide a verified and consistent single source of truth for authorised participants. 
 This structure is intended to reduce information barriers, foster fair market competition, 
and enable insurers to offer tailored products, sharper risk pricing, and improved customer 
service. 
 For policyholders, the registry promises a single -window interface to discover and 
compare coverage, verify licensed insurers, track active policies across multiple carriers, 
streamline claims, and locate forgotten or unclaimed amounts. 
 The infrastructure aims to make direct purchase and policy maintenance more cost -
efficient by reducing paper-heavy verification and onboarding steps. 
 Insurers can refine underwriting models, optimise claims servicing, and reduce regulatory 
reporting burdens. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
36 
 
 Reinsurers will gain access to standardised exposure and catastrophe insights for better 
capital allocation and risk transfer. 
 Financial institutions can instantly verify policy ownership and collateral coverage for 
credit-risk assessment. 
 Intermediaries can compare product information, use verified data for faster onboarding, 
and improve sales quality. 
 Research institutions will receive approved, anonymised datasets for market studies and 
evidence-based policymaking. 
 The system provides early-warning supervision mechanisms and visibility into nationwide 
protection gaps for regulatory purposes. 
 Irdai has invited feedback from industry players, technology architects, and the public on 
architecture, data privacy, consent frameworks, and transition timelines by September 30. 
Sebi and ESMA Sign MoU to Strengthen Regulatory Cooperation on Clearing Houses 
 Sebi (Securities and Exchange Board of India) and ESMA (European Securities and 
Markets Authority) signed a memorandum of understanding (MoU) to strengthen 
cooperation and exchange of information relating to central counterparties (CCPs) 
regulated and supervised by Sebi. 
 The pact replaces an earlier MoU between the two regulators signed on June 21, 2017. 
 It establishes a framework for cooperation between Sebi and ESMA in accordance with 
their respective laws and regulations. 
 The MoU enables ESMA to rely on Sebi's regulatory and supervisory activities. 
 The arrangement seeks to safeguard the European Union's financial stability while 
facilitating cross-border cooperation for international clearing activities. 
 The MoU aims to strengthen regulatory coordination around CCPs, which play a critical 
role in clearing and settling trades. 
 It provides a mechanism for the two authorities to share relevant information and 
cooperate on supervisory matters involving CCPs under Sebi's oversight. 
 The agreement was signed by Sebi Chairman Tuhin Kanta Pandey and ESMA Chair 
Verena Ross. 
 The development comes as cross -border regulatory cooperation gains importance with 
increasing international participation and linkages in financial markets.

IFSCA Notifies New Market Abuse Regulations for GIFT City Securities Market, Replacing 
Sebi's Existing Rules 
Key Points 
• The International Financial Services Centres Authority (IFSCA) has notified the IFSCA 
(Prohibition of Market Abuse in Securities Markets) Regulations, 2026, consolidating 
provisions on insider trading, fraudulent and manipulative practices, and unfair trading into 
a single regulatory framework for GIFT City's securities market. 
• The new rules replace the Securities and Exchange Board of India's (Sebi's) Prohibition 
of Insider Trading Regulations, 2015, and Prohibition of Fraudulent and Unfair Trade 
Practices Regulations, 2003, which will no longer apply within the International Fi nancial 
Services Centre (IFSC) once the new regulations take effect. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
38 
 
• The framework prohibits fraudulent transactions, price and benchmark manipulation, 
creation of artificial demand, circular trading, and spreading false or misleading 
information through physical or digital media. 
• It specifically targets "spoofing" — repeatedly placing and cancelling orders without 
intending to execute them, aimed at artificially altering supply, demand, or prices. 
• On insider trading, the rules bar insiders from sharing material non -public information 
except for legitimate purposes or legal duties, and prohibit trading (or enabling others to 
trade) while in possession of such information; trades made while holding such information 
will be presumed to be based on it, subject to specified defences. 
• Designated persons such as controlling shareholders and directors must disclose 
quarterly trades exceeding $25,000 within two trading days, and listed entities must notify 
stock exchanges and publish these disclosures on their websites within two working days. 
• Listed entities are also required to set up internal controls and a code of conduct covering 
confidentiality of sensitive information, restrictions on its communication, identification of 
employees with access to it, and periodic reviews of these controls. 
• For violations, the IFSCA can issue warnings or censures, and suspend or cancel the 
registration of regulated entities or persons. 
Net SIP Inflows Hit Record ₹2 Trillion in FY26 Despite Rise in Account Closures 
Key Points 
• Net systematic investment plan (SIP) inflows into mutual funds reached a record ₹2 trillion 
in FY26 (2025-26), accounting for 56% of the ₹3.5 trillion in gross SIP investments — the 
highest share in at least three years, up from 54% in FY25, according to S ebi's annual 
report. 
• Net SIP inflow is calculated by subtracting redemptions from gross SIP investments; the 
monthly SIP data released by the Association of Mutual Funds in India (Amfi) reflects only 
the gross number. 
• SIP account outflows (redemptions) stood at ₹1.5 trillion in FY26, up 15% from ₹1.3 trillion 
in FY25, marking the slowest growth in redemptions over the last three years, compared 
to 57% growth in FY24 and 18% in FY25. 
• The rise in redemptions in FY26 was significantly lower than the 21% surge in gross 
inflows recorded during FY25. 
• Net SIP account additions slowed sharply to 4 million in FY26, compared to nearly 17 
million net additions in FY25, reflecting the impact of equity market turbulence on new 
account growth even as inflows stayed strong. 
• The equity market remained volatile through the two years ending March 2026: the Nifty 
50 index ended this period down over 5%, the Nifty Smallcap 250 index stayed flat, while 
the Nifty Midcap 150 index gained 6%.

Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
39

with l aunch expected around 30 days after back -end integration between Central

• PFRDA's focus on expanding pension coverage targets India's informal workforce, which 
makes up nearly 90% of all working people, using tools such as NPS (National Pension

contributions.

asset diversification.

Sebi Launches Demat 2.0 Pilot for Tokenised Corporate Bonds Using Distributed Ledger 
Technology 
Key Points 
• The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Sebi) launched Demat 2.0, a pilot project for 
tokenising corporate bonds using distributed ledger technology (DLT), jointly unveiled by 
RBI Governor Sanjay Malhotra and Sebi Chairman Tuhin Kanta Pandey at the Glo bal 
Fintech Fest. 
• Three companies have issued tokenised bonds worth a total of ₹1,025 crore so far: REC 
raised ₹500 crore from 18 investors on September 7, L&T raised ₹500 crore from four 
investors on September 9, and IIFL raised ₹25 crore. 
• Under the pilot, corporate bonds are created as digital tokens on a distributed ledger — a 
shared electronic record maintained by market infrastructure institutions and owned by the 
depositories. 
• Sebi clarified that the bond remains the same instrument in law, with the company's 
repayment obligation and investors' rights unchanged, and that existing requirements on 
credit rating, debenture trustees, listing and disclosures continue to apply in full. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
40 
 
• The system is linked to the RBI's wholesale Central Bank Digital Currency (CBDC), the 
digital rupee, through the Unified Market Interface (UMI), enabling atomic settlement, 
where the bond and payment move simultaneously. 
• Interest payments and redemptions under the pilot can be handled automatically through 
smart contracts, aimed at making bond issuance, settlement and servicing faster and less 
error-prone. 
• The pilot is being rolled out in phases: the first phase covers ongoing issuances, while 
later phases will extend to secondary trading via existing request-for-quote platforms and 
eventually retail investor access. 
• Investors can hold these tokenised bonds in their existing demat accounts, with no 
separate account or fresh KYC (Know Your Customer) process required. 
IFSCA Pilots Aadhaar Face Authentication to Simplify Cross-Border KYC for NRIs 
Key Points 
• The International Financial Services Centres Authority (IFSCA) is piloting unassisted 
Aadhaar face authentication with four international jurisdictions to simplify the cross -
border Know Your Customer (KYC) process for Non -Resident Indians (NRIs), aiming to  
make GIFT (Gujarat International Finance Tec) City a hub for global wealth management. 
• Under the pilot, an NRI with an Aadhaar footprint can complete a fully digital, paperless 
onboarding process — without couriers or wet signatures — to open an account and invest 
from overseas. 
• IFSCA has identified cross-border KYC and payments as the two key issues that need to 
be resolved to enable NRIs to invest globally through the GIFT City platform and to invest 
in India. 
• IFSCA has directed banks operating in the International Financial Services Centre (IFSC) 
to set up wealth-management units; banks including HSBC and State Bank of India (SBI) 
have already begun establishing such units to serve high-net-worth individuals (HNIs) and 
overseas family offices. 
• NRIs remitted more than $140 billion to India last year, and IFSCA is looking to channel a 
similar pool of overseas Indian wealth into investments through GIFT City. 
• Total assets of banks operating in the IFSC are projected to rise to around $200 billion by 
the end of the financial year, up from more than $143 billion currently; there are 42 banks 
operating in the IFSC, including 21 foreign banks. 
• The IFSC is also increasingly being used for financing large assets, with Indian banks 
participating in loan syndicates, creating opportunities to support acquisitions of assets 
such as aircraft and ships.

Irdai proposes phased cuts to insurers' expense limits, new commission caps and a ban 
on compulsory bundling of insurance with loans 
Key Points 
• The Insurance Regulatory and Development Authority of India (Irdai) on September 
23, 2026 issued a consultation paper proposing a phased reduction in the expenses 
of management (EoM) limits to lower the cost of insurance, widen the risk pool in general 
insurance and improve returns to policyholders in life savings products.  
 Life insurers:  company-level EoM to fall to 15% of gross direct premium 
income (GDPI) within 2 years and 12.5% within 5 years, while insurers already 
below the benchmark in FY25 should reach 10% within 5 years, described as the 
long-term sector goal. 
 General insurers:  the EoM benchmark shifts from gross written premium 
(GWP) to domestic GDPI, and the limit falls from 30% to 20% over 5 years, with 
the same 20% five-year target for those already below the benchmark. 
• A new commission framework  would set limits by segment, line of business, 
distribution channel, product complexity and effort in selling and servicing , and 
products sold in underserved areas may earn rewards above the normal limits.  
 Life, individual non -linked and linked products:  5-20% for intermediaries 
(depending on the premium payment term) and 6.25-25% for agents. 
 Life, individual pure-term products: first-year 25% for intermediaries and 30% 
for agents, with renewal 7.5% and 10% respectively. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
45 
 
 Life, single-premium products: about 1-2% for savings products and 7.5-10% 
for pure-term. 
 Motor third-party (TP): zero commission for distributors and 2.5% for agents 
and associates. 
 Motor own-damage (OD), personal accident (PA) and legal liability (LL) on 
new vehicles: 5% for intermediaries and 10% for agents and associates. 
 Individual health insurance: first-time sales about 15% for distribution entities 
and 20% for agents, with renewals 5%  and 10% (agents and associates) 
respectively. 
• Measures to curb mis-selling:  
 Prohibit compulsory bundling  of insurance with credit or loans, while allowing 
acceptable combinations. 
 Prohibit volume-linked or reward-linked incentives for bank and NBFC staff 
selling insurance, with stronger accountability. 
 Link an individual's identity to each policy sold , put mis-selling incidents in 
the public domain  and provide for clawback of commissions  in mis -selling 
cases. 
 Create awareness through illustrations of mis-selling. 
• Transparency and cost proposals:  
 Insurers and large distributors must disclose commission policies and structures 
in simple language. 
 Cost audits  are proposed as mandatory for all insurer expenses , including 
intermediary payouts and non -monetary incentives , and for distribution 
entities with insurance revenue above ₹100 crore. 
 The regulatory fee paid by insurers is proposed to be cut from 0.05% to 0.04% 
of premium, subject to a maximum of ₹20 crore. 
• The paper also proposes Market Infrastructure Institutions (MIIs) for insurance  as 
digital, pull-based alternatives for distribution, with Bima Sugam identified as one such 
infrastructure.

Irdai's proposed commission caps may weigh on insurance fee income of banks and 
NBFCs, with L&T Finance seen as most exposed 
Key Points 
• Analysts say Irdai's proposed commission caps  could weigh on the insurance fee 
income of banks and NBFCs, with lenders that have multiple insurer tie-ups and rely 
heavily on incentive-led payouts facing a sharper impact than those with a single-tie-
up, closed-architecture model.  
 The proposed cuts in commissions on credit life and short -tenure savings 
products, along with the ban on compulsory bundling of insurance with loans, 
are expected to add pressure on distribution income. 
• Among NBFCs, L&T Finance  is seen as the most exposed , followed by Piramal 
Enterprises and Cholamandalam Investment and Finance , because their income 
comes mainly from credit-linked and motor insurance, which the draft targets through 
the bundling ban, caps on staff incentives and lower motor commissions (according 
to JM Financial).  
 L&T Finance:  insurance commission income was about 26% of FY26 profit 
before tax (PBT). 
 Piramal Finance: insurance commission income was 38.4% of PBT in FY25. 
 Poonawalla Fincorp, Cholamandalam Finance and Mahindra & Mahindra 
Financial Services also have meaningful exposure. 
• Among private sector banks, Axis Bank and HDFC Bank are more exposed than other 
large banks because fee income contributes more to their revenue and PBT (according 
to Macquarie). 
• Findings from Irdai's consultation paper on distribution:  
 Banks accounted for nearly ₹68,000 crore of corporate-agency premium in life 
insurance in the sample studied. 
 Payouts were significantly higher in multiple -tie-up arrangements  than in 
single-tie-up models, suggesting remuneration is driven more by competition for 
distribution relationships than by the effort involved. 
 NBFC-sourced new business premium nearly tripled to ₹10,300 crore in FY25 
from ₹3,600 crore in FY23, while payouts to NBFCs rose to ₹4,300 crore, about 
42% of the premium sourced. 
 Nearly 93% of this business is loan-linked group credit life, which the regulator 
considers a relatively low -effort product  as it is sold at the time of loan 
disbursement. 
 Commission on such products  rose to 28% from 5% in two years , and to 
around 45% when other payouts are included.

Sebi approves PMS-mutual fund route (PRIM), overhauls settlement rules and widens FPI 
access to non-agri commodity derivatives 
Key Points 
• The Securities and Exchange Board of India (Sebi)  board on September 24, 2026  
approved a wide -ranging set of reforms covering portfolio manager regulations, 
settlement proceedings, FPI access to commodity derivatives  and a common 
advertisement code for regulated entities. 
• Portfolio manager (PMS) reforms:  
 The Portfolio Managers Route for Investing in Mutual Fund Units (PRIM) lets 
PMS players invest client funds in direct mutual fund schemes and specialised 
investment funds (SIFs), as a separate investment approach with a minimum 
ticket size of ₹25 lakh. 
 A new category of "independent fund managers" (IFMs)  will manage client 
portfolios in association with a registered portfolio manager, subject to investor 
protection safeguards. 
 PMS players can invest up to 10% of client assets under management  in 
investment-grade unlisted debt securities  under discretionary PMS , with 
client consent, and can invest in IPOs, primary market debt issuances and 
exchange-traded derivatives. 
• Settlement proceedings overhaul:  
 Sebi will issue a settlement notice before a show -cause notice, giving entities 
60 days to apply for settlement, except where an interim order is expected. 
 There is a new formula for computing settlement amounts , with separate 
treatment for wrongful gains. 
 Settlement now extends to cases of misrepresentation of financial statements 
or diversion of funds. 
 A fast-track settlement mechanism  is introduced for disclosure-related 
violations where the settlement amount is up to ₹10 lakh. 
• FPI access to commodity derivatives:  
 FPIs can participate in non-agricultural index derivatives contracts , 
irrespective of whether the underlying is cash -settled, and in non-cash-settled 
non-agricultural commodity derivatives, subject to safeguards. 
 FPIs must exit such commodity derivatives positions before the delivery 
obligation arises. 
• Other approvals:  
 A common advertisement code for certain regulated entities permits celebrities 
for brand - or entity -level promotion  and removes certain mandatory prior 
approvals, except where celebrity endorsements are involved. 
 Depository receipts on units of REITs and InvITs were cleared to attract foreign 
capital. 
 Under the reformed accredited investor framework, persons resident outside 
India, including FPIs, will be deemed accredited investors. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
48 
 
 Changes were approved to Vault Manager regulations  and ease-of-doing-
business measures for REITs and InvITs. 
 Issuers planning a first listed NCD issuance  no longer need to list all 
outstanding unlisted NCDs. 
 After the meeting, Sebi relaxed the requirement for research analysts and 
research entities to keep call recordings  of communications with institutional 
clients. 
• Statements by Sebi chairman Tuhin Kanta Pandey:  
 Sebi has received over 3,500 comments  on its consultation on reforms to the 
Closing Auction Session (CAS). 
 The RBI is examining issues Sebi flagged on easing FPI operations, including 
faster onboarding. 
 In the margin trading facility (MTF), the top 25 scrips account for about 20% of 
the overall MTF book. 
 On UPI MDR, Sebi is looking at how to represent brokers' concerns, and brokers 
have also made a representation to the government. 
 Sebi is pushing exchanges to strengthen systems and improve governance, and 
says co-location access in the commodities segment  must be fair to all 
participants.

• The Securities Appellate Tribunal (SAT) disposed of appeals filed by five foreign portfolio 
investors (FPIs) named in the Hindenburg Research report concerning the Adani Group. 
• The five FPIs are LTS Investment Fund, Asia Investment Corporation Mauritius, APMS

• The appeals concerned Rule 4(3) of the SEBI (Procedure for Holding Inquiry and Imposing

conduct a formal inquiry.

inquiry after considering their responses to the show-cause notices. 
• SAT disposed of the appeals after SEBI agreed to provide copies of the adjudicating 
officer's file noting. The tribunal's written order is awaited.

PFRDA Issues Operational Guidelines for NPS Swasthya 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
50 
 
• The Pension Fund Regulatory and Development Authority (PFRDA) issued operational 
guidelines for NPS Swasthya in September 2026 under Section 14 of the PFRDA Act, 
2013. The guidelines came into force immediately. 
• The scheme combines retirement savings with healthcare benefits under the National 
Pension System. 
• Coverage: Subscriber, spouse and a maximum of two dependent children under a family 
floater; parents are excluded. Entry age is 18–70 years, with renewals permitted up to 85 
years, subject to policy terms. 
• Components: NPS Swasthya Investment Account and Super Top-up. 
• Minimum contributions: ₹1,000 towards the investment account initially, along with the 
first-year insurance premium and ₹200 towards annual maintenance charges, plus 
applicable taxes. Subsequent contributions can be as low as ₹10. 
• Insurance options: ₹1 lakh cover with ₹10,000 deductible; ₹5 lakh with ₹50,000 deductible; 
₹10 lakh with ₹1 lakh deductible; and ₹30 lakh with ₹3 lakh deductible. 
ICAI Issues SSA 5000 for Sustainability Assurance 
• The Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) issued SSA 5000 – General 
Requirements and Framework for Sustainability Assurance Engagements in September 
2026. 
• The standard establishes a principle-based framework covering sustainability disclosures 
related to climate, labour practices and biodiversity. 
• It aligns with the International Standard on Sustainability Assurance (ISSA) 5000, issued 
by the International Auditing and Assurance Standards Board (IAASB), with India-specific 
modifications. 
• The Indian modifications include provisions concerning joint audits and forward -looking 
statements. 
• SSA 5000 will apply from 1 April 2027, replacing SSAE 3000 and SAE 3410, which will be 
withdrawn from April 2027. 
• The framework aims to improve consistency, strengthen stakeholder confidence and 
address greenwashing.

• PayGlocal, a cross -border payments company, received in -principle approval from the 
International Financial Services Centres Authority (IFSCA) in September 2026 to establish 
a Payment Service Provider (PSP) in GIFT City, Gujarat.

accounts, cross-border money transfers and merchant acquisition, subject to the required 
authorisation. 
• The company plans to initially support Indian merchants serving international customers 
before expanding its services to businesses in other markets.
