28 September 2026. 
• SMFCL aims to raise ₹600 crore, along with a green shoe option of ₹500 crore. 
• The bond has a 10 -year tenor and will finance maritime lending, greenfield port projects 
and coastal road networks. 
• The bond issue received an AA+ rating from ICRA and CARE. 
• SBI Capital Markets has been appointed as the arranger for the issue. 
• Blue bonds are debt instruments used to finance projects related to marine ecosystems, 
sustainable fishing, clean water, shipping efficiency and ocean conservation. 
• SMFCL, incorporated in August 2016, is a Mini Ratna Category-I CPSE under the Ministry 
of Ports, Shipping and Waterways, headquartered in New Delhi. 
 
NABARD and Agriculture 
 
Agriculture and Allied Sectors Record 3.6% GVA Growth in Q1FY27, Down from 4.4% Last 
Year 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
52 
 
 Agriculture and allied activities clocked a Gross Value Added (GVA) growth of 3.6% in the 
first quarter of FY27, down from 4.4% in the same period last financial year, according to 
Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation (MoSPI) data. 
 The decline is likely due to low production in animal husbandry and livestock sectors, 
which were impacted by flooding and extreme heat from April to June. 
 At current prices, GVA growth in Q1FY27 was 7.5%, up from 4.7% in the same period last 
year. 
 In Q4FY26 (January-March), agriculture and allied activities had recorded a GVA growth 
of 3.9% at constant prices. 
 Madan Sabnavis, Chief Economist at Bank of Baroda, said the actual impact of low 
monsoon on agriculture GVA for FY27 will be felt in coming quarters. 
 Full-year agriculture and allied activities GVA in FY27 is estimated at around 3.5 -4%, in 
line with the long-term trend but with a downward bias. 
 The downward bias is due to kharif acreage remaining lower than last year despite revival 
in rains. 
 The southwest monsoon as on August 30 was almost 14% below average cumulatively 
across India. 
 Eastern and southern peninsular India recorded a deficit of almost 26% and 24%, 
respectively, from June 1 to August 30, 2026. 
 Rains were more than 36% less than normal in June, which sharply pulled down kharif 
acreages, but a strong pickup in July narrowed the deficits. 
 Kharif sowing has been completed in around 109 million hectares as on August 28, 2026, 
which is about 1.7% less than last year and almost 3% lower than normal acreage. 
 Sowing for most crops is now almost complete across India.

 National Commodity & Derivatives Exchange (NCDEX) signed a five -year strategic 
partnership with the Cotton Association of India (CAI) to strengthen participation in cotton

awareness across India's cotton value chain. 
 NCDEX's cotton derivatives platform currently includes contracts for kapas, cottonseed oil 
cake and cotton wash oil. 
 The initiative will promote derivatives trading among farmers, ginners, spinners, exporters 
and traders as a tool for managing price risks. 
 Maharashtra, Gujarat, Punjab, Andhra Pradesh and Telangana will be key focus areas for 
awareness and adoption programmes under the partnership.

PMMSY Completes Six Years on 10 September 2026 
• Pradhan Mantri Matsya Sampada Yojana (PMMSY)  completed six years on 10 
September 2026. 
• The scheme was launched in 2020 with an outlay of ₹20,050 crore  for sustainable 
fisheries development.

Pradhan Mantri Kisan Maandhan Yojana Completes Seven Years 
• PM-KMY, launched on 12 September 2019, completed seven years in 2026 and provides 
old-age social security to Small and Marginal Farmers (SMFs). 
• It is a voluntary and contributory pension scheme , implemented by the Ministry of 
Agriculture & Farmers Welfare in collaboration with LIC. 
• Eligible farmers receive a minimum assured pension of ₹3,000 per month from the 
age of 60; monthly contributions range from ₹55 to ₹200 based on entry age. 
• As of February 2026, 24.96 lakh farmers had enrolled under the scheme. 
• Farmers with cultivable landholding of up to 2 hectares  and aged 18–40 years  are 
eligible, subject to the scheme's conditions. 
SPROUT Facility Inaugurated for Climate-Resilient Crop Research 
• Union Minister Dr. Jitendra Singh inaugurated the SPROUT facility at BRIC-NIPGR, New 
Delhi. 
• SPROUT stands for SpeedSeed Phenotyping and Resource Optimization for Unified 
Trait Analysis. 
• The facility enables high-throughput phenotyping, precision stress screening and 
evaluation of germplasm, mutants and genome-edited lines. 
• Initially focused on chickpea, it integrates genomics, genome editing and phenotyping to 
accelerate crop breeding. 
• SpeedSeed technology can reduce crop cycles to around 40 days  and supports the 
BioE3 policy.

• India initiated its first farm-level soil carbon payments at Punjab Agricultural University, 
Ludhiana. 
• Around 2,550 smallholder farmers from Punjab and Haryana  will receive more than 
₹2.9 crore for adopting verified regenerative agricultural practices. 
• The payments are part of the Aadi programme, launched by Grow Indigo in 2019 with 
technical guidance from ICAR. 
• Practices included Direct Seeded Rice (DSR), reduced tillage and crop -residue 
management. 
• The first issuance covered around 30,000 acres and generated more than 50,000 verified 
carbon credits; individual farmers received approximately ₹3,000–₹15,000.

DoCA Notifies Legal Metrology (Indian Standard Time) Rules, 2026 
 The Department of Consumer Affairs notified the Legal Metrology (Indian Standard Time) 
Rules, 2026, making IST the single time reference for legal, administrative, commercial 
and other official purposes across India. 
 The Rules were notified under Section 52 of the Legal Metrology Act, 2009, through 
G.S.R. 761(E), and will come into force 180 days after publication in the Official Gazette. 
 The framework aims to reduce dependence on foreign satellite -based time sources and 
strengthen cybersecurity for critical infrastructure. 
 It is expected to ensure accurate time -stamping of banking and digital payment 
transactions and improve coordination across transport and government systems.

Western Dedicated Freight Corridor Fully Operational, To Cut Freight Transit Time by 60% 
Key Points 
• Prime Minister Narendra Modi dedicated the ₹73,000 crore Western Dedicated Freight 
Corridor (DFC) to the nation, marking the completion of a project spanning 2,800 km that 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
62 
 
was first conceived under Prime Minister Atal Bihari Vajpayee and formally launched by 
Prime Minister Manmohan Singh in 2005. 
• With both the Western and Eastern DFCs now complete, India has finished its second -
largest infrastructure project after the Golden Quadrilateral highway network. 
• The Western DFC connects Dadri in Uttar Pradesh to Jawaharlal Nehru Port Authority 
(JNPA) in Maharashtra, one of India's major export -import gateways, and is expected to 
cut freight transit time on this route by around 60%. 
• Freight trains on the corridor can run at speeds up to 100 kmph, covering 100 km in about 
two-and-a-half hours, compared to nearly six hours on conventional mixed -use railway 
tracks. 
• The final missing link to JNPA, delayed for years due to contractual issues with a Tata 
Projects-led consortium, was completed earlier this year, giving state -owned JNPA the 
ability to handle double -stacked containers like private rivals Mundra Port (Adani  Ports) 
and Pipavav Port (APM Terminals). 
• Container train operators report the corridor currently delivers only 6 -7% cost savings 
against an estimated potential of 25%, with weight and dimension regulations, additional 
port handling charges at JNPA, and congestion on feeder routes connecting conventional 
tracks to the DFC cited as key bottlenecks. 
• The Eastern DFC, completed in October 2023 at a cost of ₹51,000 crore, serves coal and 
mineral-rich states, while the two corridors combined cost ₹1.24 trillion against an original 
estimate roughly one-sixth of that amount. 
• The two DFCs together have a capacity of 480 trains a day and are currently operating 
around 435 trains a day, having carried 130,116 trains in 2024 -25 according to the 
Dedicated Freight Corridor Corporation of India (DFCCIL). 
NITI Aayog Launches PACT and ZET Marketplace 
• NITI Aayog launched the Platform for Aggregating Clean Transport (PACT) at the 5th 
e-FAST India Summit in New Delhi. 
• It also introduced the Zero-Emission Truck (ZET) marketplace. 
• PACT aims to aggregate freight demand and support deployment across freight corridors. 
• The ZET marketplace connects e-truck manufacturers, logistics providers, financiers and 
technology companies. 
PM-SETU Approves ₹735.70 Crore Investment for 3 ITI Clusters 
• The 5th National Steering Committee meeting under PM-SETU approved investments 
worth ₹735.70 crore for three ITI clusters in Rajasthan, Uttar Pradesh and Telangana.

Centre Amends E-Commerce Rules to Strengthen Consumer Protection and Transparency 
• The Department of Consumer Affairs amended the Consumer Protection (E -
Commerce) Rules, 2020  through the Consumer Protection (E -Commerce) 
(Amendment) Rules, 2026. 
• The amendments will come into force from 1 January 2027. 
• E-commerce platforms must become National Consumer Helpline (NCH) convergence 
partners and provide complainants with a copy of complaints recorded by grievance 
officers. 
• Sponsored listings must have clear disclosures, and platforms cannot manipulate search 
results to mislead consumers. 
• Platforms must display current and previous prices, with the previous price defined as the 
lowest price offered during the preceding 30 days.

Commerce Ministry Seeks Five-Year RoDTEP Extension, Higher FY27 Outlay 
Key Points 
• Commerce Ministry has sought a five-year extension of the RoDTEP scheme, which is set 
to end on September 30. 
• Higher allocation sought: 
 Department of Commerce has asked the Department of Expenditure to raise 
RoDTEP's FY27 allocation to ₹23,000 crore from the budgeted ₹10,000 crore. 
• Final decision:  Department of Expenditure will take the final call on extension and 
allocation. Commerce Ministry expects clarity by September 30. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
66 
 
• What RoDTEP does: 
 Reimburses exporters for embedded non-creditable central, state, and local levies 
paid on inputs. 
 Based on the principle that taxes should not be exported. 
 Ensures zero-rating of exports, making them competitive. 
• Background: 
 Launched in January 2021 to replace the Merchandise Exports from India Scheme 
(MEIS). 
 Designed to ensure India's export incentives comply with WTO guidelines. 
 Initially launched for 27 months (ending March 2023) with an allocation of ₹27,018 
crore. 
 Extended at least five times since March 2023, for periods ranging from six months 
to one year. 
 Last extended in April by six months amid the conflict in West Asia. 
• FY26 spending:  Government likely spent ₹18,233 crore on the scheme, per revised 
estimate in FY27 Budget. 
• Exporter concern:  Exporters have long demanded predictability. Repeated short -term 
extensions (six months to one year) create uncertainty. They want a long -term, 
sustainable extension. 
EPFO Wage Ceiling Raised to ₹25,000 for Better Social Security Net 
Key Points 
• Government has raised the EPF wage ceiling to ₹25,000 per month from ₹15,000, 
effective September 17. 
• Impact: Over 51 lakh (5.1 million) additional employees brought under mandatory EPF 
coverage. 
• Who is covered now:  Employees earning up to ₹25,000/month will now mandatorily 
come under: 
 Provident Fund savings 
 Pension eligibility under Employees' Pension Scheme (EPS) 
 Insurance protection under Employees' Deposit Linked Insurance Scheme (EDLI) 
• Financial outgo: 
 Estimated annual outgo: ₹11,339 crore (vs existing annual budgetary support of 
about ₹10,250 crore) 
 Estimated five-year expenditure: ₹56,696 crore 
• Contribution impact: 
 Employer contribution may rise by around ₹600 per employee per month on 
average 
 Compulsory pension contribution for employees could rise from ₹1,250 to roughly 
₹2,000 
• Background: 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
67 
 
 Wage ceiling remained unchanged since September 2014 (when it was raised to 
₹15,000). 
 Decision followed Supreme Court direction in January asking Centre and EPFO to 
decide within four months. 
 Trade unions had long demanded revision, some seeking ceiling up to ₹40,000. 
 Recommended by Expenditure Finance Committee on June 16, 2026. 
• EPFO data: 
 Around 7.98 crore (79.8 million) contributing members 
 About 7.68 lakh (768,000) contributing establishments 
 EPS provides pension to around 82 lakh (8.2 million) pensioners 
• Government rationale:  Average income of regular salaried employee is now around 
₹23,000/month; minimum wages for unskilled workers crossed ₹15,000 in several states. 
Old ceiling no longer reflects prevailing wage levels. 
• Benefits: Strengthens portability of social security, supports worker retention, workforce 
stability, and formalisation. 
• Concerns: 
 Employers, especially MSMEs, may find additional cost difficult to absorb. 
 Employees in ₹15,000 –25,000 bracket may see reduced take -home pay due to 
higher PF contribution. 
 Employers need to update HR and payroll systems.

• Indian Oil Corporation Ltd (IOCL)  approved an investment of ₹2,448.70 crore for the

Registration of Births and Deaths Amendment Act, 2026 to Come into Force from October 
1 
• The Registration of Births and Deaths (RBD) Amendment Act, 2026  will come into 
force from 1 October 2026. 
• The Act amends Section 13(3) of the RBD Act, 1969 , strengthening the framework for 
delayed registration. 
• Births, deaths and stillbirths are generally required to be reported within 21 days. 
• Registration delayed by more than one year but up to two years  will require approval 
from the DM, SDM or authorised Executive Magistrate. 
• Registration delayed by more than two years  will require an order from the Judicial 
Magistrate First Class (JMFC).

and operational feasibility.

FY27, 4% in FY28 and 5% from FY29 onwards.

PM Internship Scheme: only 20% of candidates who got offers joined across three rounds 
till August 2026 
Key Points 
• The PM Internship Scheme , launched in October 2024 , completes two years on 
October 3, 2026. It offers internships of 6 to 12 months with a monthly stipend of ₹9,000, 
of which the Centre bears 90%  through direct benefit transfer  and the participating 
company pays the rest. 
• Across three rounds (October 2024 to August 2026) , companies posted 400,822 
opportunities, 39% of which were converted into offers, but only 20% of the candidates 
who got offers actually joined. 
• The round-wise picture was as follows:  
 Round 1 (October 2024): 127,508 opportunities, about 621,000 applications and 
60,866 offers, with 8,760 joining (14.4%), of whom 3,731 completed and 4,698 
dropped out (dropout rate 57.4%). 
 Round 2 (February 2025): 118,948 opportunities, about 455,000 applications and 
71,192 offers, with 7,300 joining (10.3%), of whom 2,880 completed and 2,648 
dropped out (dropout rate 36.3%). 
 Round 3 (April 2026):  a record 154,366 opportunities but only 350,259 
applications and 25,794 offers, with 14,620 joining (56.7%), the highest of all three 
rounds. 
• Actual spending has fallen far short of estimates:  
 FY25: estimated ₹2,000 crore, actual only about 1.5% of the estimate. 
 FY26: estimated ₹10,831 crore, actual only about 0.9% of the estimate. 
 FY27: estimated at ₹4,788 crore. 
• An RTI response shows that of the scheme's actual allocation of ₹123.8 crore between 
FY24 and FY26 , about 10% (₹12.6 crore)  was spent on publicity by the Ministry of 
Information and Broadcasting's Bureau of Communication , split about 60% print 
media and 40% digital media.

of a ₹5,427 crore integrated iron ore processing project. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
73 
 
• The project comprises an iron ore processing plant at Bacheli, a 135 -km slurry pipeline 
with a capacity of 15 MTPA, and a 2 MTPA pellet plant at Nagarnar. 
• The facilities will process iron ore fines and slimes from the Bailadila mines into

production capacity to 100 million tonnes.

Crore 
• The Pradhan Mantri Khanij Kshetra Kalyan Yojana (PMKKKY), launched on 17 September 
2015 by the Ministry of Mines, completed 11 years in September 2026.

• The scheme aims to improve the quality of life in mining -affected areas through social 
infrastructure, environmental protection, healthcare and sustainable livelihood 
opportunities.

Regulation) Act, 1957.

communities affected by mining activities.

Biometric Aadhaar Authentication Made Mandatory for Subsidised LPG Refills from 
October 1, 2026 
• The Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas has mandated Biometric Aadhaar 
Authentication (BAA) for domestic LPG consumers from 1 October 2026 to avail 
subsidised refills at the regulated retail selling price. 
• Consumers can complete authentication during delivery through LPG delivery personnel, 
at distributor showrooms or through Oil Marketing Company (OMC) mobile applications 
such as IndianOil ONE, HelloBPCL and HP PAY. 
• Consumers who do not complete BAA can obtain 5-kg or 10-kg cylinders at market prices 
without subsidy, subject to local availability. 
• Domestic LPG is sold below its actual cost, resulting in an implicit subsidy estimated at 
approximately ₹210 per 14.2-kg cylinder in September 2026, compared with ₹721 in June 
2026.

President Amends Inter -State Council Order to Include Governors and Lt Governors 
During President’s Rule 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
75 
 
• President Droupadi Murmu amended the Inter -State Council Order, 1990, through the 
Inter-State Council (Amendment) Order, 2026, which came into force immediately upon 
publication. 
• The amendment was notified by the Ministry of Home Affairs on September 9, 2026, under 
Article 263 of the Constitution. 
• Under the amendment, the Governor of a State must be invited to Inter -State Council 
meetings when President’s Rule under Article 356 is in force in that State. 
• The Lieutenant Governor of Delhi must be invited when a Presidential order under Article 
239AB is in force in the National Capital Territory of Delhi. 
Premier Energies Commissions India’s Largest Solar Cell Facility 
• Premier Energies commissioned a 7 GW N-type TOPCon solar cell manufacturing facility 
at Naidupeta, Andhra Pradesh, increasing its total solar cell manufacturing capacity from 
3.6 GW to 10.6 GW. 
• Developed over 101 acres at a capital expenditure of ₹3,293 crore, the facility is expected 
to produce around 88,000 solar cells per hour, with an average efficiency of approximately 
25.8% after stabilisation. 
• The facility uses Tunnel Oxide Passivated Contact (TOPCon) technology, Artificial 
Intelligence and a Zero Liquid Discharge system for water recycling.

including backward integration into ingot and wafer manufacturing.

Government Mandates BIS Certification for Smartphone Screen Protectors from April 2027 
• The Ministry of Electronics and Information Technology (MeitY) mandated BIS certification 
for smartphone screen protectors, effective 1 April 2027. 
• Screen protectors must comply with Indian Standard IS 19348:2025 before they can be 
manufactured, imported or sold in India. 
• The standard covers thickness, dimensions, light transmission, visual defects and 
strength. Compliant products must carry the BIS Standard Mark. 
• Smartphone screen protectors have become the 66th product category covered under the 
compulsory registration framework. 
• The transition period for compliance ends on 31 March 2027. BIS was established on 1 
April 1987 and is headquartered in New Delhi.

Four Ports Notified as India's First Mega Ports 
• The Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways (MoPSW) notified four ports as India's first

• The four ports are Jawaharlal Nehru Port (Nhava Sheva), Paradip Port, Deendayal 
(Kandla) Port and Mundra Port. 
• Mega port status will remain valid for five years, subject to periodic review. 
• The eligibility criteria include annual cargo throughput of at least 150 million metric tonnes 
(MMT) for bulk ports or 7.5 million TEUs for container ports. 
• Mundra Port, operated by Adani Ports and Special Economic Zone, is the only privately 
owned port among the four notified ports.

completion by March 2027. 
• Deendayal Port Authority and Assam Petro-Chemicals Limited will contribute to the project 
in a 76:24 ratio, respectively. 
• The proposed production cost is around USD 750 per tonne, compared with a global rate 
of USD 1,300 per tonne.

UN Report Says Earth Will Soon Cross 1.5°C Safe Warming Limit, Urges Plan to Cool Back 
Down 
 The United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP) has released a first-of-its-kind report 
acknowledging that Earth will soon exceed the safe temperature threshold agreed in the 
2015 Paris climate accord. 
 The warming is caused by the burning of coal, oil, and gas. 
 Instead of giving up, UN scientists recommend limiting excessive warming and then 
bringing temperatures back down to the agreed threshold. 
 UN Secretary-General Antonio Guterres said humanity is speeding past the limit to avoid 
climate catastrophe. 
 The world is currently at 1.4°C above pre-industrial levels, based on a 20-year average. 
 The report admits the world will cross the 1.5°C threshold within the next few years. 
 The most optimistic scenario has warming peaking at 1.8°C around mid -century if 
countries pursue ambitious climate goals. 
 Current government policies could push temperatures to 2.6°C above pre-industrial levels 
by 2100. 
 The new strategy is called &quot;overshoot&quot; — crossing the 1.5°C limit, peaking, 
then gradually cooling back below 1.5°C, ideally by the end of the century. 
 UNEP Executive Director Inger Andersen said the 1.5°C target is still the goal but must 
now be approached differently. 
 Every five years of delay in reducing emissions adds at least a tenth of a degree to peak 
warming. 
 During the overshoot period, more people will die or fall sick, food production will drop, 
water shortages will worsen, economies will be hurt, and species like coral will go extinct. 
 The report warns of &quot;tipping points&quot; with irreversible effects, including loss of 
West Antarctic and Greenland ice sheets, loss of the Amazon rainforest, and shutdown of 
the Atlantic Ocean circulation system. 
 Some locations may become uninsurable or uninhabitable. 
 The final phase involves cooling the planet by removing carbon dioxide from the air and 
storing it. 
 Planting trees could at most reduce global temperatures by a tenth of a degree, so more 
novel carbon removal approaches will be needed. 
 Some scientists called these approaches unreliable and expensive. 
 Richard Betts is a climate scientist at the University of Exeter and a co-author of the report. 
 Joeri Rogelj is a climate scientist at Imperial College London and a co-author. 
 Andrew Weaver is a climate scientist at the University of Victoria. 
 Rob Jackson is a climate scientist at Stanford University. 
 Bill Hare is CEO of Climate Analytics. 
Dutch Central Bank Moves 86 Tonnes of Gold to London in 'Crisis Preparedness' Move 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
82 
 
 The Dutch central bank (DNB) announced it moved billions of dollars worth of gold 
reserves out of North America as a &quot;crisis preparedness&quot; measure amid global 
political unrest. 
 Between March and August, about 86 metric tons of gold were transferred to London from 
New York and Ottawa, Canada. 
 Before the move, New York housed 31.3% of the Dutch gold and Ottawa held 19.7%. 
 After the move, both New York and Ottawa now hold 18.5% each. 
 The bank owns 612.4 metric tons of gold, worth €72.2 billion (about $83.6 billion) at the 
end of 2025. 
 More than 27 metric tons of gold were physically transferred from the US and Canada to 
the Dutch bank's vault on a military base near Zeist. 
 A similar quantity was transferred from Zeist to London. 
 The rest of the relocation was done by selling around 59 metric tons of gold in New York 
and using the proceeds to buy gold in London. 
 DNB Governor Olaf Sleijpen said the relocation has improved the tradability of the gold 
reserves and strengthened resilience and preparedness. 
 The bank said gold held at the Bank of England meets modern international trade 
standards and is regarded as the world's most easily tradable gold. 
 Gold reserves held in New York and Ottawa cannot be utilised as quickly and directly in a 
crisis situation. 
Japanese Companies in India Hit Record 1,463 as India-Japan Economic Ties Deepen 
 The number of Japanese -owned companies operating in India surged to a record 1,463 
in 2026, up from 1,374 in 2025, according to a Grant Thornton Bharat report titled

 India-Japan bilateral merchandise trade doubled over the past decade, rising from $13.60 
billion in FY2016-17 to $27.48 billion in FY2025-26. 
 Japanese cumulative foreign direct investment (FDI) in India stood at $48.14 billion 
between April 2000 and March 2026. 
 FDI equity inflows from Japan amounted to $3.75 billion in FY2025 -26, accounting for 
6.36% of India's total FDI equity inflows. 
 Japanese-owned companies in India employ about 459,333 individuals, up from 365,252 
in 2025. 
 These companies operate across technology, automobiles, business services, industrial 
products, consumer, retail, e-commerce, and financial services. 
 The largest concentration of Japanese companies is in the National Capital Region (NCR), 
followed by Maharashtra, Karnataka, and Tamil Nadu. 
 Japan's exports to India are concentrated in high -value capital goods, machinery, 
industrial inputs, and technology-intensive intermediates. 
 India's exports to Japan are more diversified, including vehicles, auto components, 
organic chemicals, machinery, marine products, and others. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026

 The Comprehensive Economic Partnership Agreement (CEPA) provides the institutional

digital technologies.

investment.

infrastructure, and critical minerals is expected to drive the next phase of the India-Japan 
economic partnership. 
World Food Prices Rise in August to Highest Since Late 2022: FAO 
 World food prices rose in August to their highest level since late 2022, according to the 
United Nations' Food and Agriculture Organization (FAO). 
 Adverse weather and war disruption in the Black Sea heightened concerns over the supply 
of staples. 
 Extreme heat and drought in Europe, the threat of a severe El Nino weather pattern, and 
trade upheaval caused by the Ukraine and Iran wars unsettled agricultural markets. 
 Grain prices rose to three-year highs, while sugar reached a one-year peak. 
 The FAO Food Price Index, which tracks monthly changes in a basket of internationally 
traded food commodities, averaged 133.3 points in August, up from July's revised reading 
of 130.8. 
 This was the highest score since November 2022, though nearly 17% below the record 
peak from March 2022. 
 The FAO's price benchmarks for cereals, vegetable oils, sugar, meat, and dairy all rose in 
August. 
 FAO Chief Economist Maximo Torero said the August increase is a warning that the risk 
premium is returning to food markets. 
 Torero added that climate shocks, geopolitical tensions, and disrupted trade logistics are 
converging to tighten supply expectations.

UNGA Adopts ‘Correct the Map’ Resolution 
 The UN General Assembly (UNGA) adopted the “Correct the Map” resolution with 164 
votes in favour. 
 The resolution promotes the Equal Earth projection for more accurate representation of 
the relative sizes of countries and continents. 
 It is a non -binding resolution and does not ban the Mercator projection, which remains 
useful for navigation. 
 The initiative highlights distortions in traditional maps, including the fact that Africa is about 
14 times larger than Greenland.

India's Brics Trade Deficit Widens to $226 Billion in FY26, Driven Mainly by China, UAE and 
Russia 
Key Points 
• Ahead of the 18th Brics Summit hosted by New Delhi, data shows India's trade with its 10 
Brics partners is heavily import -driven, with the bloc accounting for nearly 42% of India's 
merchandise imports in FY26 (2025-26) but only 22% of its exports. 
• India imported goods worth $321.8 billion from Brics countries against exports of just $95.7 
billion, resulting in a trade deficit of $226.1 billion, which made up nearly 68% of India's 
total merchandise trade deficit of $334.3 billion for the year. 
• China, the UAE, and Russia together accounted for nearly 84% of India's trade deficit with 
Brics nations, supplying goods worth $250.9 billion — almost a third of India's total 
merchandise imports in FY26. 
• China alone contributed nearly half of India's Brics trade deficit, with imports of $131.6 
billion against exports of only $19.5 billion, leaving a gap of $112.2 billion; Commerce 
Minister Piyush Goyal is expected to raise this widening deficit with his Ch inese 
counterpart during the summit. 
• Russia was the second-largest contributor to the deficit at $50.9 billion, a gap that widened 
after the Russia -Ukraine war began in February 2022, when Russia started offering 
discounted crude oil to India. 
• The UAE, with which India has a Free Trade Agreement (FTA), created a deficit of $26.5 
billion due to large crude-oil imports, even though it remains India's top export destination 
among Brics nations. 
• India recorded a trade surplus with only three Brics partners in FY26 — Egypt, Ethiopia, 
and Iran. 
Brics Flags 'Unilateral' Tariffs and Trade Barriers as Risks to Global Economy 
Key Points 
• Brics finance ministers and central bank governors, in a joint statement after a two -day 
meeting in Mumbai, raised concerns over unilateral trade and finance -related measures, 
including higher tariffs and non -tariff barriers, calling them inconsistent with  World Trade 
Organization (WTO) rules. 
• The statement said these pressures weigh most heavily on emerging markets and 
developing economies (EMDEs), while asserting that Brics economies have shown 
resilience and continue to contribute significantly to global growth. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
88 
 
• The 11-member Brics grouping — including Brazil, Russia, India, China and South Africa 
— reaffirmed support for an "open, transparent, inclusive, non -discriminatory, and rules-
based" multilateral trading system with the WTO at its core. 
• Brics called for the immediate implementation of quota increases for EMDEs at the 
International Monetary Fund (IMF), as agreed under the 16th General Review of Quotas 
(GRQ), to protect the quota shares of the poorest member countries. 
• The joint statement said any voluntary financial contributions to the IMF should not 
influence quota allocation, governance representation, or voting power. 
• On cross-border payments, Brics acknowledged work on interoperability of payment and 
messaging channels and encouraged the Brics Payment Task Force (BPTF) to continue 
developing fast, low -cost, and secure cross -border payment solutions among member 
countries, while stopping short of a full consensus on local-currency trading. 
• Recognising the potential of artificial intelligence (AI) and quantum computing for the 
financial sector, Brics adopted an EMDE-centric approach to assess related opportunities 
and risks, and encouraged the Brics Fintech Working Group to continue policy di alogue 
on these technologies. 
• Other key demands from the joint statement included: reforms giving developing 
economies greater voice at the IMF and World Bank; expansion of the New Development 
Bank (NDB) for more development financing and local-currency lending; a new Investment 
Platform and a proposed Brics Risk Lab; and joint cooperation on climate finance and 
financial-sector cybersecurity. 
PhonePe to Launch Operations in UAE as First International Market 
• PhonePe will launch its first international operations in the United Arab Emirates (UAE). 
• The company has secured two licences from the Central Bank of the UAE  to offer 
consumer payment instruments and merchant e-payment acceptance infrastructure. 
• Services may include wallets, gift cards, consumer payments and merchant acquiring. 
• The expansion could facilitate remittances between India and the UAE  and payments 
for UAE-based users. 
• UAE's domestic payment network Aani is already connected with India's UPI network, 
supporting cross-border payment opportunities.

• Google (Alphabet) will invest at least €13 billion ($15.1 billion)  in AI infrastructure in 
Finland during 2027–2028, its largest European investment. 
• The investment includes developing three new data centres in northern Finland. 
• Google signed a 22-year nuclear power purchase agreement for up to 50% of the output 
of one Finnish nuclear plant. 
• The deal is expected to contribute approximately €3.6 billion to Finland's GDP  during 
construction and support around 7,000 jobs annually once operational. 
• Google and Fortum will also explore developing new nuclear and renewable energy 
projects in Finland.

India and MERCOSUR Sign Protocol for Paperless Trade 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
92 
 
• India and MERCOSUR (Southern Common Market)  signed the First Additional 
Protocol to the India –MERCOSUR Preferential Trade Agreement (PTA) in September 
2026. 
• The protocol enables acceptance of electronic Certificates of Origin (e-CoO) and aims 
to modernise customs and preferential trade procedures. 
• It amends Article 16 of Annex III (Rules of Origin) of the India–MERCOSUR PTA, giving 
eligible e-CoOs the same legal validity as paper certificates. 
• The agreement also covers areas including AI, High-Performance Computing (HPC)  
and a Double Taxation Avoidance Agreement (DTAA). 
• The protocol was signed by Rajesh Agarwal of the Department of Commerce along with 
representatives of Uruguay and Paraguay.

soil health, food safety, agroforestry and sustainable forest management ; the 10th 
AIMMAF will be held in 2028.

NASA Discovers ‘McGetchin’ Crater on the Moon 
• NASA scientists confirmed the discovery of the McGetchin crater on the Moon, formed 
by an asteroid or comet impact in May 2024. 
• The crater was identified using imagery from NASA’s Lunar Reconnaissance Orbiter 
(LRO) and is named after scientist Thomas McGetchin. 
• It measures approximately 222 metres in diameter and 43 metres in depth. 
• The impact created a 7-km-wide cold spot, with debris ejected more than 100 km from 
the impact site. 
• Such large-scale impacts are considered rare, occurring approximately once every 132 
years, according to the source.

India close to concluding four bilateral investment treaties (BITs) under an updated 
framework, with the draft awaiting Cabinet approval 
Key Points 
• A Ministry of Finance official said India is close to concluding four bilateral investment 
treaties (BITs) based on an updated BIT framework, with the UK, the European Union 
(EU), Oman and Qatar, and has recently started negotiations with Canada.  
 The draft of the updated BIT framework  is with the Union Cabinet  and is 
expected to be approved very soon. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
95 
 
 Union Commerce Minister Piyush Goyal said on September 25, 2026 that India 
and Australia are working towards a BIT. 
• The BIT push comes amid a slump in foreign direct investment (FDI): net FDI inflows 
fell from an annual average of $40 billion between FY20 and FY22  to $6.95 billion in 
FY26.  
 Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman  announced a review of the BIT 
framework in her FY26 Budget speech , saying the model BIT would be 
revamped and made more investor-friendly. 
• Background on India's BIT framework:  
 The current model BIT was rolled out in 2015 , and in 2016 the government 
unilaterally cancelled BITs with over 50 partners. 
 The requirement to exhaust local legal remedies for five years  before starting 
international arbitration made it hard to conclude new treaties, and this clause is 
likely to remain in the updated model, though sources say the five-year timeline 
may be eased. 
 Department of Economic Affairs Secretary Anuradha Thakur said the dispute 
settlement clause is being reviewed along with other provisions. 
• A live dispute under the old framework:  
 Malaysia's Dhaya Maju Infrastructure (Asia) Sdn Bhd issued a claim notice of 
over ₹1,200 crore under the India-Malaysia BIT over alleged judicial delays in 
India. 
 Its Indian SPV, Jabalpur Corridor (India) Pvt Ltd, was set up for a road project 
in Madhya Pradesh , and after the Madhya Pradesh Road Development 
Corporation terminated the concession, an arbitral award  was passed in the 
SPV's favour in August 2014. 
 The company says that 12 years later the SPV has received only about 50% of 
the awarded amount. 
 The India-Malaysia BIT was among those terminated in 2016, but as the award 
came in August 2014, while the treaty was in force, the company is proceeding 
under it. 
• India is also easing FDI norms:  
 The government recently eased FDI norms for inventory -based e-commerce 
firms. 
 In May, under Press Note 2 of 2026 , India allowed investment without 
government approval  from entities with non-controlling land -bordering 
country ownership of up to 10%.

2026, held in Houston, USA, from 14–16 September 2026.

India–New Zealand FTA to Come into Force on 20 October 2026 
• Union Commerce and Industry Minister Piyush Goyal announced that the India –New 
Zealand Free Trade Agreement (FTA) will come into force on 20 October 2026. 
• The agreement was signed on 27 April 2026 at Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi, by Piyush 
Goyal and New Zealand's Trade and Investment Minister Todd McClay. 
• Under the agreement, 100% of India's exports to New Zealand will receive duty -free 
access. 
• Key beneficiary sectors include textiles and apparel, leather and footwear, gems and 
jewellery, engineering goods and processed food. 
• Indian manufacturers will also receive duty -free access to selected inputs, including 
wooden logs, coking coal and metal scrap.

Bihar and Jharkhand Resolve 25-Year-Old Sone River Water Dispute 
 Bihar and Jharkhand signed an MoU to resolve their nearly 25 -year-old dispute over 
sharing the waters of the Sone River. 
 Of the 7.75 Million Acre-Feet (MAF) allocated under the 1973 Bansagar Agreement, Bihar 
will receive 5.75 MAF and Jharkhand 2 MAF. 
 The dispute arose after Jharkhand was carved out of Bihar in November 2000. 
 The agreement marks the fourth inter-state water agreement in 2026.

Chhattisgarh Tops Jal Sanchay Jan Bhagidari 3.0 
 Chhattisgarh secured first position nationally in the third phase of the Jal Sanchay Jan 
Bhagidari (JSJB 3.0) initiative under the Ministry of Jal Shakti's Catch the Rain campaign.

Punjab Launches ‘Pehchaan’ Portal for Unidentified Deceased Persons 
 Punjab launched ‘Pehchaan’, a statewide digital portal to identify and trace unidentified 
and unclaimed deceased persons in government mortuaries. 
 Punjab became the first state in India to implement such a system. 
 The portal is built on the Medico -Legal Examination and Post-Mortem Reporting System 
(MedLEaPR) developed by NIC. 
 It stores photographs and identification details within 48 hours and integrates with the 
Crime and Criminal Tracking Network & Systems (CCTNS).

Preparedness through Up-skilling of New Generation Youth (NIPUN) Mission.

Delhi Govt Doubles Monthly Incentive for ASHA Workers to ₹6,000 
Key Points 
• Delhi government has approved doubling the monthly incentive for ASHA workers from 
₹3,000 to ₹6,000. 
• Decision approved in a Cabinet meeting chaired by Delhi Chief Minister Rekha Gupta. 
• ASHA workers in Delhi: 6,725 currently working. 
• Coverage: Each ASHA worker is generally associated with around 2,000 people or 400 
families. 
• Role of ASHA workers: 
 Visit households to provide health-related information 
 Encourage families to get vaccinated 
 Assist with care of pregnant women and new mothers 
 Provided key healthcare services during the Covid pandemic 
• Significance of the hike: 
 Recognises ASHA workers' contribution 
 Ensures their financial security 
 Boosts work enthusiasm and continuity of services 
 Strengthens primary healthcare services 
 Promotes women's economic empowerment

Six States Sign Agreement for Kishau Multipurpose Project 
• The Chief Ministers of Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand, Himachal Pradesh, Rajasthan, 
Delhi and Haryana signed the agreement for the Kishau Multipurpose Project in New 
Delhi. 
• The project is aimed at advancing the long -pending development project and supporting 
the rejuvenation of the Yamuna River. 
• Kishau Corporation Limited , a Himachal Pradesh –Uttarakhand joint venture, will 
implement the project, subject to Union Cabinet approval. 
• Declared a National Project, the Centre will bear 90% of the financial burden, while the 
six states will share the remaining 10%. 
• The project includes a 232.6-metre-high concrete gravity dam on the Tons River with a 
storage capacity of 1,562 MCM. 
Kumik Declared Ladakh’s First Heritage Village 
• Ladakh Lieutenant Governor Vinai Kumar Saxena declared Kumik village in the Zanskar 
region as Ladakh’s first Heritage Village during the 11th Ladakh Zanskar Festival 2026. 
• The recognition highlights Kumik’s Buddhist manuscripts, traditional architecture and 
cultural practices. 
• Kumik is located in the Zanskar region of Kargil district, a high-altitude cold desert area 
known for Tibetan Buddhist traditions. 
• The village preserves traditional mud architecture, sacred spaces, agricultural 
practices, community customs and rare Kanjur manuscripts. 
• The Heritage Village concept promotes “living heritage”, where cultural practices and 
knowledge systems continue to remain active within the community.

 MoUs signed rose 12.82% from 3,900, and the estimated MoU value  jumped

Delhi Cabinet Approves DURGA Scheme for 1,300 Pink E-Autos 
• The Delhi Cabinet, chaired by Chief Minister Rekha Gupta, approved the DURGA (Driving 
Upliftment and Rozgar for Women/Transgender Green E -Auto) Scheme in September 
2026. 
• The first phase will provide 1,300 pink electric autos, comprising 1,170 for women and 130 
for transgender persons. 
• Eligible women must be 20 –40 years old, residents of Delhi and possess a voter ID, an 
LMV driving licence and a Public Service Vehicle badge. 
• Applicants must have a family income of up to ₹5 lakh for 2025 –26 and must not own a 
registered transport vehicle. Women with more than three children are ineligible. 
• The vehicles will have a three -year lock -in period and cannot be sold or transferred. 
Applications will be submitted online.

farmers.

Reports and Indexes 
 
India’s Manufacturing PMI Falls to 5-Year Low in August 2026 
 HSBC India Manufacturing Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI) declined to 52.8 in August 
2026 from 53.5 in July, marking its lowest level in five years. 
 The PMI remained above the 50 -mark, indicating continued expansion in manufacturing 
activity; a reading below 50 indicates contraction. 
 New orders and output growth slowed to their weakest pace in five years, reflecting weaker 
demand and sales growth. 
 Manufacturing employment contracted for the first time in around two -and-a-half years, 
indicating a slowdown in hiring. 
 August 2026 marked the 59th consecutive month of expansion in India’s manufacturing 
sector, although the PMI remained below its long-run average of 54.2. 
SBI Research Raises India's FY27 GDP Growth Forecast to 7.3% 
 SBI Research raised India's GDP growth projection for FY 2026 –27 to 7.3%, from its 
earlier estimate of 6.6%. 
 The revision follows India's strong 7.8% GDP growth in Q1 FY27. 
 The report projected real GDP growth at 7.3% in Q2, 7.2% in Q3 and 6.9% in Q4. 
 Strong domestic demand, government capital expenditure, services and manufacturing 
were identified as key growth drivers.

Accounts Statistics (NAS) 2026 publication, adopting 2022–23 as the base year. 
 The publication contains 60 statements covering final estimates for FY23 and FY24 and 
the First Revised Estimates for FY25. 
 It covers macroeconomic aggregates, production, value added, consumption, savings, 
capital formation, public sector and external transactions.

Higher Education (AISHE) for 2022–23 and 2023–24. 
 The number of universities increased from 760 in 2014 –15 to 1,289 in 2023 –24, while

participation than males.

 In June 2026, 18 out of 19 sub -sectors recorded positive year -on-year growth, while Air

 Real Estate was the fastest -growing sub-sector at 24.7%, followed by Retail Trade and 
Wholesale Trade.

India's Per Capita Protein Consumption Projected to Triple by 2070, Raising Climate 
Concerns: ARE-FICCI Report 
Key Points 
• A report by Asia Research and Engagement (ARE), in partnership with the Federation of 
Indian Chambers of Commerce and Industry (Ficci), projects that India's average annual 
per capita protein consumption will more than triple from 9.5 kg in 2025 to 30 kg by 2070, 
driven by population growth, rising incomes, urbanisation, public nutrition programmes 
and changing dietary preferences. 
• Despite this rising consumption, the report notes that India remains protein insecure and 
continues to face dietary inequality across the population. 
• Under a business-as-usual (BAU) scenario, India's annual protein-related emissions could 
reach 1.92 gigatonnes of carbon dioxide equivalent (Gt CO2e) by 2070, more than seven 
times the estimated climate-safe threshold of 0.25 Gt CO2e. 
• A combined approach involving production efficiency gains across dairy, poultry and 
seafood systems, along with diversifying protein sources, could reduce annual emissions 
to 0.30 Gt CO2e by 2070 — still above the climate -safe threshold but a substantial 
improvement over the business-as-usual projection. 
• Dairy and pulses are expected to remain India's main protein sources through 2070, with 
eggs, poultry and seafood growing from smaller current bases, while red meat is projected 
to stay a relatively minor part of the country's protein basket. 
• India currently imports nearly 90% of its processed plant -protein isolates, a figure the 
report flags as both a supply -chain vulnerability and an opportunity to expand domestic 
processing and value addition. 
Haryana Leads with Seven Most Productive Informal-Sector Districts: NSO's First District-
Level Estimates 
Key Points 
• Haryana has seven districts among India's top 50 most productive districts in the informal 
non-agricultural economy, ranked by gross value added (GVA) per worker for market 
establishments, according to the first-ever district-level estimates released by the National 
Statistics Office (NSO). 
• Maharashtra, Tamil Nadu and Telangana follow with six districts each, with districts from 
just eight states accounting for 40 of the top 50 most productive districts nationally. 
• The estimates are drawn from the Annual Survey of Unincorporated Sector Enterprises 
(ASUSE) 2025, whose redesigned sample allowed the NSO to publish figures for 757 
individual districts across all states and Union Territories for the first time. 
• Satara district in Maharashtra recorded the highest output per worker at ₹8.7 lakh, 
followed by Fatehgarh Sahib in Punjab and Tonk in Rajasthan, against a national average 
value added per worker of ₹1.56 lakh. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
108 
 
• On women's participation in the informal workforce, the top 50 districts by female worker 
share are concentrated in Telangana (nine districts), Manipur and Mizoram (eight each), 
and Meghalaya and Tamil Nadu (six each). 
• Nirmal in Telangana has the highest female workforce share, with women making up more 
than three -quarters of informal workers, followed by Imphal East in Manipur and 
Nizamabad in Telangana. 
• At the lowest end, women make up just 6.7% of informal workers in Rudraprayag, 
Uttarakhand, followed by Marigaon in Assam (9.5%) and Banaskantha in Gujarat (10.7%). 
• Nationally, women form a third of the informal workforce in 237 districts and an outright 
majority in 25 districts; in Mizoram, female-owned proprietary establishments account for 
at least 50% of establishments in every district of the state. 
• The unincorporated non-agricultural sector shows sharp geographic concentration: the top 
10 districts (spread across Gujarat, Telangana, Uttar Pradesh and West Bengal) account 
for about one -tenth of total establishments, workers and GVA, while the top 50 d istricts 
capture nearly one-third of the total establishments, workforce and GVA in the sector. 
India's Electronics Import Dependence on China Rises to 71 Product Lines in FY26: Study 
Key Points 
• China supplied at least 80% of India's imports across 71 electronics product lines in 2025-
26 (FY26), with dependence concentrated in core upstream components such as motors, 
cables, and switching equipment rather than finished goods, according to a study by the 
Koan Advisory Group and the Institute of Chinese Studies. 
• The number of such high-dependence product lines rose from 44 in 2018-19 to 71 in 2025-
26, an increase of more than 60%, with 46 of these lines crossing the 80% threshold only 
after 2018-19. 
• The study examined bilateral trade under Harmonized System (HS) Chapter 85, which 
covers electrical machinery and electronic equipment, the internationally standardised 
code used to classify traded goods. 
• India's overall trade deficit with China reached $112.1 billion in FY26, of which electrical 
machinery alone accounted for $43.1 billion — around 38% of the total; HS Chapter 85 is 
India's largest export category to China at $3.18 billion, but this is far outweighed by 
imports in the same category. 
• India's lithium-ion battery imports from China more than doubled since 2021 -22 to $3.9 
billion, with China's share rising to 83.6% in FY26, crossing the 80% dependence 
threshold for the first time. 
• China supplied 48.9% of India's semiconductor imports, a decline from around 64% a year 
earlier. 
• The report noted that India's trade deficit with China is structural rather than cyclical, 
remaining concentrated in the same major HS chapters over time, and stated that 
restricting imports cannot resolve this dependence since the gap lies in upstream 
components rather than finished products. 
• The study called for policy to prioritise localisation of upstream component manufacturing, 
supported by India's critical -minerals strategy, along with a calibrated framework to 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
109 
 
selectively allow Chinese manufacturers to produce within India in segments where 
domestic alternatives remain unviable.

Bengal. 
 
World Economic Forum Global Gender Gap Index 2026 
• The World Economic Forum (WEF)  released the 20th Global Gender Gap Report 
2026, with India retaining the 131st position among 145 countries. 
• India’s overall gender parity score improved marginally to 0.645, while Iceland retained 
the top position for the 17th consecutive year. 
• The Index measures gender gaps across economic participation, education, health 
and survival, and political empowerment. 
• Globally, 69.2% of the gender gap  has been closed, while gender parity at the current 
rate of progress is estimated to take 120 years. 
• India’s gender parity improved by 4.3 percentage points since 2006 , with educational 
attainment being a major contributor.

• NITI Aayog released the 2nd edition of the India Electric Mobility Index (IEMI) 2025 , 
covering 36 States and UTs for January–December 2025. 
• Delhi retained the top position with a score of 84, followed by Maharashtra (78)  and 
Karnataka (73). 
• The index was developed by NITI Aayog in partnership with World Resources Institute 
(WRI) India. 
• It uses 16 indicators  across three areas: Transport Electrification Progress (50%), 
Charging Infrastructure Readiness (30%) and EV Research & Innovation (20%). 
• Among categories, Maharashtra topped Large States, Manipur topped Hilly & 
Northeastern States, while Delhi, Chandigarh and Goa led the UTs and City States.

WEF Chief Economists Outlook: India and South -East Asia the only regions where most 
economists expect real incomes to rise 
Key Points 
• The World Economic Forum's Chief Economists Outlook  (survey conducted in 
August 2026 , released September 22 ) found that most of the 36 participating 
economists expect inflation-adjusted incomes to fall or stagnate  in nearly every 
geography, with India and South -East Asia  the exceptions, where over 60%  of 
respondents expect real incomes to rise.  
 Respondents expect household costs to rise across the board, led by food (88%), 
electricity (83%) and transport (77%). 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
111 
 
 Governments are expected to respond mostly with broad measures: tax cuts on 
essential goods (60%), consumption subsidies (54%) and price caps (50%), 
while only 36% expect tax cuts for low -income households and 26% expect 
targeted cash transfers. 
• India retained the strongest growth outlook of any geography in the survey:  
 98% of economists expect moderate or stronger growth  over the next 12 
months, and 74% expect strong or very strong growth, up from 52% in the May 
edition. 
 The FY27 growth forecast was raised to 6.7% in August from 6.5% in May, on 
resilient domestic demand, though higher energy prices continue to weigh on the 
outlook. 
• The domestic data backdrop is as follows:  
 GDP growth: the economy grew 7.8% in Q1 FY27 (April-June), beating the RBI's 
7% projection, against 6.9% a year earlier and 8.6% in the March quarter ( NSO 
data, August 31). 
 CPI inflation: rose to 4.82% in August from 4.45% in July, a 20-month high on 
a back-series basis and above the RBI's 4% median target for the third straight 
month. 
 WPI inflation: rose to 9.92% in August from 9.78% in July, its second-highest 
print in 29 months, with pressure concentrated in energy. 
 Unemployment: the jobless rate for those aged 15 and above eased to a five-
month low of 5% in August  from 5.1% in July , as both the LFPR and WPR 
strengthened. 
 Equities: the Nifty 50 was down 7.9% since the start of the year as of August 19. 
• Expectations on policy and jobs in the survey:  
 Inflation: 55% expect moderate inflation in India and 45% expect high inflation, an 
improvement from May, when 61% anticipated high or very high inflation. 
 Monetary policy: 67% expect the RBI to hold policy unchanged over the coming 
year and 24% expect tightening. The RBI has kept the repo rate at 5.25% for four 
consecutive reviews, and its next MPC meeting is on October 5-7. 
 Fiscal policy: 72% expect no change and 22% expect looser policy. 
 Unemployment: about 70% expect India's unemployment rate to hold steady, 
17% expect an increase and 13% a decrease. 
IEA: India's electrification rate could rise from 19% to 32% by 2035, led by transport 
Key Points 
• The International Energy Agency (IEA), in its latest Electrification special report, says 
India's electrification rate  could rise from 19% today to 32% by 2035 , with transport 
electrification playing a major role, while in Southeast Asia it could rise from 25% to 
37%.  


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
112 
 
 Electric two- and three-wheelers and cars are now cheaper on average over 
the usual ownership period than internal combustion engine alternatives in these 
countries. 
• Fuel-importing countries could cut their energy import bills by more than $400 billion 
by 2035 (compared with 2025) if they substantially accelerate electrification, and for India 
the savings could be nearly 30% of its 2025 import bill. 
• The West Asia conflict and disruptions to shipping through the Strait of Hormuz raised 
concerns over supply shortages and higher energy prices, and several countries 
announced electrification policies.  
 India launched a programme to procure and deploy 500,000 induction cooktops. 
• India's EV adoption data:  
 Electric car sales  rose by over 115% in the four months from March to June 
2026, compared with the same period in 2025. 
 EVs accounted for over 65% of three-wheeler sales in 2025. 
• With solar PV  shares growing rapidly and demand increasingly concentrated in the 
evening and at night , the IEA says smart charging, time -of-use tariffs and vehicle -
to-grid systems  can shift demand to off-peak periods, improving grid utilisation and 
strengthening the economic case for EVs. 
• Air conditioning demand is set to grow: India and Southeast Asia's combined share  
of the global AC stock could rise from under 10% today to 20% by 2035.  
 By 2035, around half of households are expected to have cooling access, with 
nearly 90% of new residential AC stock  added in emerging market and 
developing economies. 
• Electricity demand in emerging market and developing economies grew at about 5% 
annually from 2015 to 2025  in both India and Southeast Asia , while China alone  
accounted for over 60% of the global increase in electricity demand over the decade. 
UN Women and UN DESA Release The Gender Snapshot 2026 
• UN Women and the United Nations Department of Economic and Social Affairs (UN 
DESA) released Progress on the Sustainable Development Goals: The Gender Snapshot 
2026 on 17 September 2026. 
• The report draws on more than 100 data sources to assess progress on gender equality 
for women and girls across all 17 Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). 
• It highlighted that approximately one in four gender equality institutions experienced cuts 
in operating budgets, while nearly one in ten lost control over their budgets. 
• Between 2023 and 2024, bilateral aid with gender equality objectives declined by 13%, 
while funding for women's rights organisations fell by 28%. 
• Women's representation in national parliaments increased from 22.3% in 2015 to 27.4% 
in 2026, while their representation in local government stood at 35.6%. 
WMO Releases State of Global Water Resources 2025 Report 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
113 
 
• The World Meteorological Organization (WMO) released its State of Global Water 
Resources 2025 report in September 2026, highlighting continued global glacier mass 
loss. 
• 2025 marked the fourth consecutive year in which all major glaciated regions recorded a 
net loss in ice mass. 
• Global glacier mass loss in 2025 was estimated at 408 ± 132 gigatonnes, equivalent to a 
sea-level rise of 1.1 ± 0.4 millimetres. 
• Mountain glaciers lost approximately 6.2% of their total ice mass between 2000 and 2025. 
• Between 2023 and 2025, glaciers lost a cumulative 1,400 gigatonnes of ice, equivalent to 
around 560 million Olympic-sized swimming pools.

Commerce in India.

Africa, and the Americas.

CBRE to Set Up Global Agentic AI Centre of Excellence in Hyderabad 
 Global commercial real estate consultancy CBRE will establish its first Indian Agentic AI 
Centre of Excellence (CoE) in Hyderabad to transform its global enterprise workflows. 
 The CoE will be co-anchored between India and Dallas, Texas, with India forming the core 
of the initiative. 
 CBRE aims to automate 30 critical enterprise workflows over the next 9 –12 months,

innovation. 
NIT Rourkela Develops Hybrid Energy System to Extend EV Battery Life 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
116 
 
 Researchers at NIT Rourkela have developed and patented a hybrid energy storage 
system combining a conventional EV battery with a supercapacitor. 
 The supercapacitor handles sudden bursts of power during acceleration, deceleration and 
regenerative braking, reducing high-current stress on the battery. 
 The system can help improve battery efficiency and operating life while maintaining a 
stable 48-volt output. 
 It is designed primarily for low-voltage EVs operating in the 24–60 volt DC range, including 
electric scooters, motorcycles, e-rickshaws and cargo tricycles. 
 The technology uses a simplified architecture with fewer components and a single control 
system, and the researchers have secured a patent for the innovation. 
ISRO Launches EOS-05, India’s First Imaging Satellite in Geosynchronous Orbit 
 ISRO successfully launched EOS -05, India’s first dedicated imaging satellite in 
geosynchronous orbit, using the GSLV-F17 rocket from Sriharikota. 
 Weighing 2,367 kg, EOS-05 is India’s heaviest Earth observation satellite so far and was 
placed in its intended sub-geosynchronous transfer orbit around 18 minutes after lift-off. 
 EOS-05 will enable persistent and high-frequency observation of large regions, helping in 
monitoring weather systems, floods, forest fires, agriculture and natural resources. 
 The satellite is capable of advanced imaging across visible, infrared, multispectral and 
hyperspectral bands. 
 GSLV-F17 was the 19th flight of India’s GSLV, while the mission marked the 107th launch 
from Sriharikota. 
NASA Launches Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope 
 NASA launched the USD 4.3 billion Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope aboard 
SpaceX's Falcon Heavy rocket from Florida, USA. 
 The telescope is named after Nancy Grace Roman, NASA's first Chief of Astronomy, 
popularly known as the “Mother of Hubble.” 
 It will operate near Lagrange Point 2, around 1.6 million km from Earth. 
 The mission has a planned lifespan of 5 years, with a goal of operating it for up to 10 
years.

New Caecilian Species ‘Gegeneophis chandgadi’ Discovered in Maharashtra 
• A multi-institutional team led by the Zoological Survey of India (ZSI)  discovered a new 
subterranean amphibian species, Gegeneophis chandgadi, in Maharashtra. 
• The species was discovered at Buzawade in Chandgad, Kolhapur district, and its name 
is derived from the Chandgadi dialect of Marathi. 
• It is a caecilian, a worm-like, legless amphibian adapted to living largely underground. 
• Molecular analysis along with morphological and meristic studies established it as a 
distinct species. 
• The discovery takes the number of Gegeneophis species in India to 12, while India now 
has 458 amphibian species, including 41 caecilian species.

• Scientists identified a new wild cat species, ‘Leopardus tilcayo’ , in Bolivia’s Yungas 
cloud forest. 
• The discovery marks the first formally described new living feline species in more than 
100 years, according to the source. 
• The findings were published in Current Biology on 17 September 2026. 
• The species, locally known as Tilcayo, belongs to the tiger -cat group and was identified 
through genetic research. 
• Genetic analysis indicates that the lineage diverged from related tiger cats around 1.4 
million years ago.

India Inaugurates 51st Doppler Weather Radar at Sambalpur, Odisha 
• Union Minister of State (Independent Charge) for Earth Sciences Dr Jitendra Singh 
virtually inaugurated India's 51st Doppler Weather Radar (DWR) at Jamadarpali in 
Sambalpur, Odisha, in September 2026. 
• With this inauguration, Odisha has three Doppler weather radars, located at Sambalpur, 
Gopalpur and Paradip. 
• The radar uses the Doppler effect to track precipitation and wind patterns, supporting 
rainfall forecasting, storm monitoring and short-term weather predictions. 
• It provides observations within a 250 -km radius, with 150 -metre resolution and weather 
data updates every 10 minutes, covering 13 districts in western Odisha and neighbouring 
regions. 
• The radar supports Mission Mausam, launched in September 2024, which aims to make 
India weather-ready and climate -smart through improved forecasting and early -warning 
systems.

Government Dissolves Digital Communications Commission After 37 Years 
• The Government of India dissolved the Digital Communications Commission (DCC), the 
apex inter -departmental decision -making body under the Department of 
Telecommunications (DoT). 
• The DoT issued the resolution on September 19, 2026, which took effect upon publication 
in the Gazette of India on September 21, 2026. 
• Established as the Telecom Commission in April 1989, it was renamed the DCC in October 
2018 under the National Digital Communications Policy, 2018. 
• Its financial decision-making framework included DoT Secretary approval for projects up 
to ₹100 crore, Telecom Minister approval up to ₹500 crore and referral of projects worth 
₹500–1,000 crore to the Expenditure Finance Committee. 
• At its final meeting on September 3, 2026, the DCC finalised satellite communication 
spectrum decisions, including a five -year administrative assignment, extendable by two 
years, and a 5% Spectrum Usage Charge on Adjusted Gross Revenue.

dedicated campus-based astronomical observatory, inaugurated on September 22, 2026.

to promote observational astronomy, astrophysics research, student training and public 
outreach. 
• The observatory features a 14 -inch Celestron C14 EdgeHD Schmidt -Cassegrain 
telescope, along with advanced imaging equipment and a computerised equatorial mount. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
126 
 
• The facility was developed with scientific guidance from the Indian Institute of Astrophysics 
(IIA), Bengaluru. 
• NIT Srinagar was established in 1960 and is located on the western banks of Dal Lake in 
Jammu and Kashmir. 
DRDO Signs Agreement with Zero mK India to Develop 20 mK Dilution Refrigerator 
• The Defence Research and Development Organisation (DRDO) signed its first high-value 
deep-tech project agreement under the Technology Development Fund (TDF) Scheme 
with Zero mK India Private Limited in September 2026. 
• The project involves the indigenous development of a 20 millikelvin (mK) dilution 
refrigerator for quantum computing applications. 
• The initiative is the first high -value project under the ₹500 crore corpus approved by 
Defence Minister Rajnath Singh. 
• A dilution refrigerator is a specialised cryogenic system capable of achieving extremely 
low temperatures. A temperature of 20 mK equals approximately 0.02 Kelvin. 
• The project will be monitored by Dr Meena Mishra, Director of DRDO's Solid State Physics 
Laboratory. 
• The initiative supports the National Quantum Mission and aims to reduce India's 
dependence on imported cryogenic infrastructure.

establish an Artificial Intelligence Centre of Excellence on its campus.

Department will serve as the centre's physical location, with TCS as the Knowledge 
Partner. 
• The centre aims to strengthen industry –academia collaboration, applied research and 
workforce development. 
• Its focus areas include Natural Language Processing, Computer Vision, Image Processing 
and Data Analytics. 
• The initiative will also support internships, innovation and AI startups, aligning with Digital 
India and Viksit Bharat 2047. 
New Flowering Plant Species Begonia neelamorum Discovered in Arunachal Pradesh 
• A new flowering plant species, Begonia neelamorum, was formally documented in Keyi 
Panyor district, Arunachal Pradesh, in September 2026. 
• The discovery was published in the internationally peer-reviewed journal Taiwania. 
• The plant was found along the Keyi River at an elevation of approximately 1,000 metres. 
Its type specimen was collected on 24 May 2026. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
127 
 
• The species was documented by Dipankar Borah, Madhusudhan Khanal, Momang Taram 
and Neelam Gap. 
• It is a 10–20 cm tall tuberous plant with a single light-green leaf and white male and female 
flowers. 
• Named after the Neelam clan of the Nyishi community, it is provisionally classified as Data 
Deficient (DD) under IUCN criteria. 
TDB Signs ₹200 Crore Agreement with Agnikul Cosmos for Reusable Launch Vehicle 
• The Technology Development Board (TDB) under the Department of Science and 
Technology signed a ₹200 crore agreement with Agnikul Cosmos Private Limited in 
September 2026 to develop the Agnibaan Reusable Launch Vehicle (RLV). 
• The funding will be provided through Optionally Convertible Debentures under the 
Research Development and Innovation (RDI) Fund. 
• The project aims to advance the technology from Technology Readiness Level 4 to Level 
8, enabling high-frequency and repeatable launch missions. 
• The reusable vehicle will feature a lightweight upper stage, descent propulsion and 
controlled recovery, powered by a semi -cryogenic liquid propulsion system with deep -
throttling and restart capabilities. 
• TDB also signed an agreement to provide ₹63.84 crore to GalaxEye Space Solutions 
under the RDI Fund. 
IIT Delhi Develops India's First Indigenous Working Micro-GPU 
• Researchers at IIT Delhi developed India's first working and demonstrable indigenous 
micro-Graphics Processing Unit (micro-GPU) in September 2026. 
• The project was developed under the guidance of Professors Jayadeva and Kaushik Saha 
from the Electrical Engineering Department. 
• The micro -GPU uses Register Transfer Language (RTL) and a Spartan -7 Field -
Programmable Gate Array (FPGA) for prototyping and validating chip designs. 
• Its potential applications include industrial displays, low -cost Human-Machine Interfaces, 
e-rickshaw dashboards, inland navigation systems, e-readers and embedded visualisation 
systems. 
• The team is developing an 8 –16-core vector-style GPU architecture and a 65 nm ASIC 
proof of concept to support indigenous graphics chip production.

• The Telecom Regulatory Authority of India (TRAI) launched the Digital Connectivity Rating 
(DCR) Platform in September 2026. 
• The platform enables users to search and compare the digital connectivity ratings of 
properties under the Rating of Properties for Digital Connectivity Regulations, 2024, 
amended in May 2026. 
• Users can search for rated properties by name, location or certificate ID and verify digitally 
signed certificates through QR codes.

consumers.

certification and publication of ratings.

TakeMe2Space to Launch India's First Orbital Data Centre in 2028 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
129 
 
• Indian spacetech company TakeMe2Space announced plans to launch India's first 
networked orbital data centre mission in late 2028. 
• The mission will use SpaceX's Falcon 9 rocket under a launch service agreement signed 
with French space-tech company RIDE! Space. 
• The company plans to develop a constellation of six satellites over the coming years. 
• The initial mission will deploy two 100 -kg My Orbital Infrastructure (MOI) satellites into a 
sun-synchronous orbit at an altitude of approximately 500 km. 
• The satellites will be launched as part of the Waymaker 2 rideshare mission, operated by 
Satellite & Extraterrestrial Operations & Procedures (SEOPS). 
• Each satellite will have 10 NVIDIA Thor GPUs, 100 terabytes of storage and 1.5 kW of 
power. 
• Optical inter-satellite links will enable data and workload sharing, supporting networked 
in-orbit computing. 
• TakeMe2Space was founded in 2023 and is headquartered in Hyderabad, Telangana.

India and Belgium Sign Defence Pact, Agree to Double Bilateral Trade in Five Years 
 India and Belgium announced a major defence pact as part of 10 outcomes after talks 
between Prime Minister Narendra Modi and Belgian counterpart Bart De Wever in New 
Delhi. 
 The defence pact includes joint weapons production and provides a framework for military 
training, officer exchanges, research and development, joint exercises, and maritime -
security cooperation. 
 Half a dozen agreements were signed between private and public sector defence 
companies of both countries. 
 India will appoint a resident defence attache at its embassy in Brussels; Belgium had 
already posted its defence attache in New Delhi earlier this year. 
 Both countries intend to double bilateral trade in the next five years from $13.01 billion in 
FY26. 
 They aim to diversify trade beyond diamond commerce by leveraging the India-European 
Union (EU) free trade deal. 
 An investment fast -track mechanism will be set up to facilitate Belgian investments in 
India. 
 The India-Belgium Business Forum will be institutionalised. 
 Cooperation will deepen in ports management, mining, processing of critical minerals, 
clean energy, and semiconductors. 
 A mobility agreement is being finalised to help Indian nurses work in Belgium. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
130 
 
 Belgium's sovereign wealth fund and India's National Investment and Infrastructure Fund 
(NIIF) will collaborate to establish an Indo-Belgian economic and investment corridor. 
 Key defence deals include Exail Belgium and Larsen & Toubro building mine 
countermeasure vessels, and Thales Belgium assembling 70 mm rockets in India with 
Kalyani Strategic Systems. 
 New Lachaussée will supply a $58.1 million ammunition production line. 
 Anglo Belgian Corporation and Kirloskar Oil Engines will develop a 6 MW marine diesel 
engine for Indian naval programmes. 
 John Cockerill Defence will supply the turret for India's Zorawar light tank. 
 OIP Sensor Systems will cooperate with India's Optel on electro -optical and fire -control 
systems for the Arjun tank. 
 India and the EU concluded negotiations on the free trade agreement on January 27 this 
year in New Delhi; it now requires ratification by EU member-states. 
 Modi is expected to visit Brussels in December for the signing of the India-EU trade deal. 
 A agreement was signed between the Central Bureau of Investigation of India and the 
Federal Police of Belgium to enhance cooperation in tracing fugitives. 
 Fugitive businessman Mehul Choksi is in a Belgian jail, and Belgium is making efforts to 
extradite him to India. 
 Both sides encouraged airline operators to explore direct air connectivity between Belgium 
and India. 
 De Wever's visit is the first by a Belgian PM in two decades and builds on the Belgian 
Economic Mission to India in March 2025. 
 Theo Francken is Belgium's Defence and Foreign Trade Minister, and Sanjay Seth is 
India's Minister of State for Defence.

Indian Navy Commissions Indigenous Diving Support Vessel ‘Nipun’ 
 The Indian Navy commissioned its second indigenous Diving Support Vessel (DSV), 
‘Nipun’, at Naval Dockyard, Mumbai, in August 2026. 
 Designed and built by Hindustan Shipyard Limited, the Nistar -class vessel has around 
75% indigenous content. 
 It supports saturation diving up to 300 metres and is equipped with ROVs capable of 
operating up to 1,000 metres. 
 ‘Nipun’ can also serve as a mother ship for the Deep Submergence Rescue Vessel 
(DSRV) to rescue personnel from distressed submarines.

Russia Test-Fires RS-24 Yars Mobile ICBM 
 Russia successfully test-fired the RS-24 Yars, a mobile, solid-fuel Intercontinental Ballistic 
Missile (ICBM), from the Plesetsk Cosmodrome. 
 The missile travelled over 6,700 km and delivered training warheads to the Kura Test 
Range in the Kamchatka region. 
 The RS -24 Yars is a nuclear -capable, three -stage ICBM equipped with Multiple 
Independently Targetable Re-entry Vehicles (MIRVs). 
 It has a strike range exceeding 10,000 km and strengthens Russia's strategic nuclear 
deterrence capabilities.

avionics, navigation and mission systems were validated during trials.

precision-attack missions.

Ministry of Defence Signs ₹586 Crore Contract with AICPL for Tank Auxiliary Power Units 
• The Ministry of Defence signed a ₹586 crore contract with Accurate Industrial Controls 
Private Limited (AICPL) for the supply of 3,257 Auxiliary Power Units (APUs) for the Indian 
Army's T-72 and T-90 tanks. 
• The contract covers 1,657 APUs for T-90 tanks and 1,600 for T-72 tanks and was signed 
in the presence of Defence Secretary Rajesh Kumar Singh. 
• The indigenously developed APUs provide electricity to critical systems, including fire 
control, firing circuits and gun stabilisation, when the main engine is switched off. 
• The APUs can operate in temperatures ranging from −50°C to +45°C and are being 
developed under the Make-II category of the Defence Acquisition Procedure. 
• The project aims to strengthen the operational readiness of the Indian Army's mechanised 
formations and promote self-reliance in defence manufacturing.

professional cooperation.

• The exercise is being conducted from 28 September to 11 October 2026.

Garhwal Rifles, along with personnel from other arms, services and the Indian Air Force. 


 
 
 
Monthly Beepedia September 2026 
139 
 
• Kazakhstan has deployed a contingent of matching strength, mainly comprising personnel 
from its Land Forces. 
• The exercise focuses on joint counter -terrorism operations in semi -urban and 
mountainous terrain. 
• Training includes joint planning, air mobility, the use of unmanned aerial systems (UAS), 
counter-UAS operations, raids, search-and-destroy operations and cordon operations. 
• The exercise also involves establishing a Joint Command Post and Intelligence Centre. 
HAL and SkyPulse Sign MoU for ₹2,491 Crore Helicopter Programme 
• Hindustan Aeronautics Limited (HAL) and SkyPulse Solutions IFSC Private Limited signed 
an MoU in September 2026 for a proposed helicopter programme worth ₹2,491 crore. 
• The programme covers the phased acquisition of 30 HAL -manufactured helicopters, 
comprising single-engine and twin-engine models. 
• The acquisition is planned in two phases: 10 helicopters during 2027 –2029 and 20 
helicopters from 2030 onwards, with induction beginning in FY 2027 –28, subject to 
approvals. 
• The framework includes financing, leasing and asset management through finance 
leases, operating leases and sale-and-leaseback arrangements. 
• The helicopters may be deployed for Helicopter Emergency Medical Services (HEMS), 
disaster response, search and rescue, tourism, UDAN, government operations and 
charter services. 
• The programme also covers maintenance, repair and overhaul (MRO), training, 
engineering and spare-parts support. 
• HAL was established in 1940 and is headquartered in Bengaluru, Karnataka. Its CMD is 
Ravi K.

innovative, practical and scalable solutions. 
• It is organised by the Ministry of Education, AICTE and UGC, with IISc Bengaluru serving 
as the nodal institute. 
• The challenge has three participation levels: Junior (Classes 6 –12), Technical (ITI and

Prosperous Bharat, Healthy and Inclusive Bharat, Educated and Skilled Bharat, and 
Secure Bharat.

held in New Delhi in December 2026.

India International Water Week 2026 – 22–26 September 
• The Ministry of Jal Shakti organised the 9th India International Water Week (IIWW 2026) 
at Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi, from 22 to 26 September 2026. 
• The event brought together participants from 150 countries to discuss sustainable, 
technology-driven and climate-resilient water management. 
• It was inaugurated by Vice President C. P. Radhakrishnan. 
• 2026 theme: Climate Resilient Water Management. 
• The event was conceptualised in 2012 by the Government of India and is organised by 
the Department of Water Resources, River Development and Ganga Rejuvenation.

International Water Week from its ninth edition in 2026.

India–Africa roundtable on South–South cooperation to achieve Sustainable Development 
Goal 6 (SDG 6).
